\displaystyle\tilde{C}=\bar{C}/\sim\;=\big((\rho\cos\theta,\rho\sin\theta)\times[-1,1]\big)/\sim.

(253)

Define a map

\displaystyle h\big((\rho\cos\theta,\rho\sin\theta),t\big)=\left((\rho_{1}\cos\theta,\rho_{1}\sin\theta),\;\big(\rho_{2}\cos(t\pi+\pi),\rho_{2}\sin(t\pi+\pi)\big)\right),

(254)

where
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}>0
are constants (e.g.,
\rho_{1}=\rho
,
\rho_{2}=1
) and
t\in[-1,1]
. Since

\displaystyle\cos(t\pi+\pi)\big|{t=-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cos(0)=1,\quad\sin(t\pi+\pi)\big|{t=-1}=\sin(0)=0,

\displaystyle\cos(t\pi+\pi)\big|{t=1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cos(2\pi)=1,\quad\sin(t\pi+\pi)\big|{t=1}=\sin(2\pi)=0.

(255)

Thus
h
takes the same value at
t=-1
and
t=1
, so it descends to a well-defined map on the quotient
\tilde{C}
.

Let
\varphi(t)=(\cos(t\pi+\pi),\sin(t\pi+\pi))
. Then
\varphi:[-1,1]\to S^{1}
is surjective and
\varphi(-1)=\varphi(1)
, hence it induces a homeomorphism
[-1,1]/{-1,1}\cong S^{1}
. Therefore

\displaystyle h(\tilde{C})=\left((\rho_{1}\cos\theta,\rho_{1}\sin\theta),(\rho_{2}\cos(t\pi+\pi),\rho_{2}\sin(t\pi+\pi))\right)\cong S^{1}\times S^{1},

(256)

which is the toroidal solenoid. Thus the infinite solenoid is homeomorphic to a toroidal solenoid after adding the points at infinity and gluing them together.
\blacksquare

V.3
Brief Review of the Vector Potential Extraction Approach for the Non-Abelian Case

For the non-Abelian case, the approach is similar. The difference is that now the components of
\vec{G}
are non-abelian operators. For a simple example, let

\displaystyle q\vec{G}=\vec{S},

(257)

where
\vec{S}
is a spin-
s
angular momentum operator
(s=0,1/2,1,3/2,\cdots)
. In this situation, it is easy to have that the resultant vector

\displaystyle\vec{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{r}\times\vec{p}+q\vec{G}=\vec{r}\times\vec{p}+\vec{S}

(258)

is also an angular momentum operator. Then due to the
vector potential extraction approach
, we can derive the previous spin vector potential

\displaystyle\vec{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c\dfrac{\vec{r}\times\vec{G}}{r^{2}}=\frac{c}{q}\dfrac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r^{2}}.

(259)

Then we explain the form of spin vector potential in Eq. (
136
) with

\displaystyle\tilde{k}=\frac{c}{q}.

(260)

V.4
Determining the Most General Spin Vector Potential Based on VPEA

From previous section, we have known that the spin vector potential takes the following form

\displaystyle\vec{A}

\displaystyle\propto

\displaystyle\dfrac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r^{2}},

(261)

which comes form the angular momentum operator

\displaystyle\vec{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}+\vec{S}=\vec{r}\times\vec{p}+\vec{S}

(262)

by the
vector potential extraction approach
. It gives rise to natural question: Is Eq. (
261
) the most general form of the spin vector potential
\vec{A}
? Mathematically, from the viewpoint of VPEA, it is equivalent to ask: Given the orbital angular momentum operator
\vec{\ell}=\vec{r}\times\vec{p}
and the spin angular momentum operator
\vec{S}
, and we define

\displaystyle\vec{L}=F(\vec{\ell},\vec{S})

(263)

as an operator function of
\vec{\ell}
and
\vec{S}
, then what is the most general form of
\vec{L}
if it is still required to be an angular momentum operator? In the next subsection, let us study the general form of
\vec{L}=F(\vec{\ell},\vec{S})
.

V.4.1
The General Form of
\vec{L}=F(\vec{\ell},\vec{S})

Frankly speaking, the expression of (
262
) is obtained by a simple “displacement” (i.e.,
\vec{L}=\vec{\ell}+\vec{S}
), which is merely a simple realization of
\vec{L}=F(\vec{\ell},\vec{S})
. Based on two facts: (i)
\vec{\ell}
and
\vec{L}
are angular momentum operator, and (ii) the operator
\vec{L}
is generated from the operator
\vec{\ell}
, thus the other way to construct
\vec{L}
is to introduce the unitary transformation
\mathcal{U}
(i.e., a “rotation”). Explicitly, one has

\displaystyle\vec{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}\,\vec{\ell}\,\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}.

(264)

Such a unitary transformation acting on
\vec{\ell}
automatically guarantees that
\vec{L}
is an angular momentum operator.

Since we expect that the spin vector potential depends only on the position operator
\vec{r}
and the spin operator
\vec{S}
(i.e., it does not depend on the momentum operator
\vec{p}
), thus the unitary matrix
\mathcal{U}
is a function of
\vec{r}
and
\vec{S}
. In this work, for simplicity, let us restrict to spin-1/2 case, then we may write

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}={\rm e}^{{\rm i}\theta\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}},

(265)

with

\displaystyle\vec{S}=\frac{\hbar}{2}\,\vec{\Gamma},\;\;\;\;\hat{r}\equiv\hat{e}_{r}=\frac{\vec{r}}{r},

(266)

\vec{\Gamma}\equiv\vec{\sigma}
is the vector of Pauli matrices, and

\displaystyle(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})^{2}=\openone.

(267)

Because

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}={\rm e}^{{\rm i}\theta\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}}=\cos\theta\openone+{\rm i}\sin\theta(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}={\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\theta\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}}=\cos\theta\openone-{\rm i}\sin\theta(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}),

(268)

we can have

\displaystyle(\cos\theta\openone+{\rm i}\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\sin\theta)\,\vec{\ell}\,(\cos\theta\openone-{\rm i}\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\sin\theta)

(269)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}\cos^{2}\theta+(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\,\vec{\ell}\,(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\sin^{2}\theta+{\rm i}[\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r},\;\vec{\ell}]\sin\theta\cos\theta.

Because

\displaystyle[\ell_{z},\;\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\ell_{z},\;\Gamma_{x}\frac{x}{r}+\Gamma_{y}\frac{y}{r}+\Gamma_{z}\frac{z}{r}\right]=\frac{1}{r}\left[\ell_{z},\;\Gamma_{x}x+\Gamma_{y}y+\Gamma_{z}z\right]=\frac{1}{r}\left[\ell_{z},\;\Gamma_{x}x+\Gamma_{y}y\right]

(270)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\left\{\Gamma_{x}\left[\ell_{z},\;x\right]+\Gamma_{y}\left[\ell_{z},\;y\right]\right\}=\frac{1}{r}\left[({\rm i}\hbar)\Gamma_{x}y-({\rm i}\hbar)\Gamma_{y}x\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar(\vec{\Gamma}\times\hat{r})_{z}=-{\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})_{z},

then we have

\displaystyle[\vec{\ell},\;\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}]=-{\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}),

(271)

or

\displaystyle[\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r},\;\vec{\ell}]={\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}).

(272)

Because

\displaystyle(\vec{\Gamma}\times\hat{r})_{z}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}(\Gamma_{x}y-\Gamma_{y}x)(\Gamma_{x}x+\Gamma_{y}y+\Gamma_{z}z)=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[\Gamma_{x}y(\Gamma_{x}x+\Gamma_{y}y+\Gamma_{z}z)-\Gamma_{y}x(\Gamma_{x}x+\Gamma_{y}y+\Gamma_{z}z)\right]

(273)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[(yx+{\rm i}\Gamma_{z}y^{2}-{\rm i}\Gamma_{y}yz)-(-{\rm i}\Gamma_{z}x^{2}+xy+{\rm i}\Gamma_{x}xz)\right]=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[{\rm i}\Gamma_{z}(x^{2}+y^{2})-{\rm i}z(\Gamma_{y}y+\Gamma_{x}x)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[{\rm i}\Gamma_{z}(x^{2}+y^{2}+z^{2})-{\rm i}z(\Gamma_{x}x+\Gamma_{y}y+\Gamma_{x}x)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\Gamma_{z}-{\rm i}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{e}_{z},

then we have

\displaystyle(\vec{\Gamma}\times\hat{r})(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\vec{\Gamma}-{\rm i}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}.

(274)

Consider that

\displaystyle(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\;\vec{\ell}\;(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}-[\vec{\ell},\;\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}](\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})=\vec{\ell}-{\rm i}\hbar(\vec{\Gamma}\times\hat{r})(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})

(275)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}+\hbar\left[\vec{\Gamma}-(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}\right],

we can have

\displaystyle\vec{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\cos\theta+{\rm i}\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\sin\theta)\,\vec{\ell}\,(\cos\theta-{\rm i}\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\sin\theta)

(276)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}\cos^{2}\theta+(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\,\vec{\ell}\,(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\sin^{2}\theta+{\rm i}[\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r},\;\vec{\ell}]\sin\theta\cos\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}\cos^{2}\theta+\left\{\vec{\ell}+\hbar\left[\vec{\Gamma}-(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}\right]\right\}\sin^{2}\theta-\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})\sin\theta\cos\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}-\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})\sin\theta\cos\theta+\hbar\left[\vec{\Gamma}-(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}\right]\sin^{2}\theta,

or in terms of
\vec{S}
and
\vec{r}
as

\displaystyle\vec{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}-2\sin\theta\cos\theta\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r}+2\sin^{2}\theta\left[\vec{S}-\frac{(\vec{S}\cdot\vec{r})\vec{r}}{r^{2}}\right].

(277)

Remark 23
.

From the viewpoint of displacement, Eq. (
277
) can be written as

\displaystyle\vec{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}+q\vec{G},

(278)

with

\displaystyle q\vec{G}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sin\theta\cos\theta\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r}+2\sin^{2}\theta\left[\vec{S}-\frac{(\vec{S}\cdot\vec{r})\vec{r}}{r^{2}}\right].

(279)

(i) When
\theta=\pi/4
, one has
2\sin\theta\cos\theta=1
,
2\sin^{2}\theta=1
, and

\displaystyle\vec{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}-2\sin\theta\cos\theta\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r}+2\sin^{2}\theta\left[\vec{S}-\frac{(\vec{S}\cdot\vec{r})\vec{r}}{r^{2}}\right]

(280)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}-\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r}+\left[\vec{S}-\frac{(\vec{S}\cdot\vec{r})\vec{r}}{r^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}+\vec{S}-\left[\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r}+\frac{(\vec{S}\cdot\vec{r})\vec{r}}{r^{2}}\right],

which is different from
\vec{L}=\vec{\ell}+\vec{S}
by an additional term
-\left[\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r}+\frac{(\vec{S}\cdot\vec{r})\vec{r}}{r^{2}}\right]
. This also means that the “rotation”
\mathcal{U}\,\vec{\ell}\,\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}
cannot generate the “displacement”
\vec{L}=\vec{\ell}+\vec{S}
.

(ii) When
\theta=\pi/2
, one has
2\sin\theta\cos\theta=0
,
2\sin^{2}\theta=2
, and

\displaystyle\vec{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}-2\sin\theta\cos\theta\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r}+2\sin^{2}\theta\left[\vec{S}-\frac{(\vec{S}\cdot\vec{r})\vec{r}}{r^{2}}\right]

(281)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}+2\vec{S}-2\frac{(\vec{S}\cdot\vec{r})\vec{r}}{r^{2}}=\vec{\ell}+2\vec{S}-2(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r},

which is also an angular momentum operator, but is different from
\vec{L}=\vec{\ell}+\vec{S}
.
\blacksquare

Remark 24
.

Based on (i) the simple “displacement”
\vec{L}=\vec{\ell}+\vec{S}
, and (ii) the result obtained from the “rotation”

\vec{L}=\vec{\ell}-2\sin\theta\cos\theta\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r}+2\sin^{2}\theta\left[\vec{S}-\frac{(\vec{S}\cdot\vec{r})\vec{r}}{r^{2}}\right]
, we can find that the most general displacement is of the following form

\displaystyle\vec{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}+a_{1}\vec{S}+a_{2}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}+a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S}),

(282)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\vec{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}+q\vec{G},

(283)

with

\displaystyle q\vec{G}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}\vec{S}+a_{2}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}+a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S}),

(284)

namely,
q\vec{G}
is a linear combination of
\vec{S}
,
(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}
, and
(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})
, here
a_{1}
,
a_{2}
,
a_{3}
are some coefficients, which will be determined by the following definition of angular momentum operator

\displaystyle\vec{L}\times\vec{L}={\rm i}\hbar\vec{L}.

(285)

The Calculation.—
For simplicity, let us denote

\displaystyle\vec{K}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q\vec{G}=a_{1}\vec{S}+a_{2}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}+a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S}).

(286)

Without loss of generality, we check the
z
-component, i.e., and we can have

\displaystyle(\vec{L}\times\vec{L})_{z}=[L_{x},L_{y}]=[\ell_{x}+K_{x},\ell_{y}+K_{y}]=[\ell_{x},\ell_{y}]+[\ell_{x},K_{y}]+[K_{x},\ell_{y}]+[K_{x},K_{y}].

(287)

(i) For the first term, we can have

\displaystyle[\ell_{x},\ell_{y}]={\rm i}\hbar\ell_{z}.

(288)

(ii) For the second term,we can have

\displaystyle[\ell_{x},K_{y}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\ell_{x},a_{1}S_{y}+a_{2}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{y}{r}+a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right]

(289)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{2}\left[\ell_{x},(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{y}{r}\right]+a_{3}\left[\ell_{x},(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right].

Because

\displaystyle[\ell_{x},(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})]=\frac{1}{r}[\ell_{x},S_{x}x+S_{y}y+S_{z}z]=S_{y}\frac{{\rm i}\hbar z}{r}+S_{z}\frac{-{\rm i}\hbar y}{r}={\rm i}\hbar(\vec{S}\times\hat{r})_{{x}}=-{\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{{x}},

(290)

\displaystyle\left[\ell_{x},\frac{y}{r}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\left[\ell_{x},y\right]={\rm i}\hbar\frac{z}{r},

(291)

then we have

\displaystyle\left[\ell_{x},(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{y}{r}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\ell_{x},(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})]\frac{y}{r}+(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\left[\ell_{x},\frac{y}{r}\right]=\left[-{\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{{x}}\right]\frac{y}{r}+(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r}){\rm i}\hbar\frac{z}{r}.

(292)

Because

\displaystyle\left[\ell_{x},(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\left[\ell_{x},zS_{x}-xS_{z}\right]=\frac{1}{r}\left(-{\rm i}\hbar yS_{x}\right)=-{\rm i}\hbar S_{x}\frac{y}{r},

(293)

then we have

\displaystyle[\ell_{x},K_{y}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{2}\left[\ell_{x},(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{y}{r}\right]+a_{3}\left[\ell_{x},(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right]

(294)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{2}\left\{\left[-{\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{{x}}\right]\frac{y}{r}+(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r}){\rm i}\hbar\frac{z}{r}\right\}+a_{3}\left[-{\rm i}\hbar S_{x}\frac{y}{r}\right].

(iii) For the third term, we can have

\displaystyle[K_{x},\ell_{y}]=-[\ell_{y},K_{x}],

(295)

and

\displaystyle[\ell_{y},K_{x}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\ell_{y},a_{1}S_{x}+a_{2}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{x}{r}+a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x}\right]

(296)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{2}\left[\ell_{y},(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{x}{r}\right]+a_{3}\left[\ell_{y},(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x}\right].

Because

\displaystyle[\ell_{y},(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})]=\frac{1}{r}[\ell_{y},S_{x}x+S_{y}y+S_{z}z]=S_{x}\frac{-{\rm i}\hbar z}{r}+S_{z}\frac{{\rm i}\hbar x}{r}={\rm i}\hbar(\vec{S}\times\hat{r})_{y}=-{\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y},

(297)

\displaystyle\left[\ell_{y},\frac{x}{r}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\left[\ell_{y},x\right]=-{\rm i}\hbar\frac{z}{r},

(298)

then we have

\displaystyle\left[\ell_{y},(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{x}{r}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\ell_{y},(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\right]\frac{x}{r}+(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\left[\ell_{y},\frac{x}{r}\right]

(299)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[-{\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right]\frac{x}{r}+(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\left(-{\rm i}\hbar\frac{z}{r}\right).

Because

\displaystyle\left[\ell_{y},(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\left[\ell_{y},yS_{z}-zS_{y}\right]=\frac{1}{r}\left(-{\rm i}\hbar xS_{y}\right)=-{\rm i}\hbar S_{y}\frac{{x}}{r},

(300)

then we have

\displaystyle[K_{x},\ell_{y}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-[\ell_{y},K_{x}]=-a_{2}\left[\ell_{y},(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{x}{r}\right]-a_{3}\left[\ell_{y},(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x}\right]

(301)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-a_{2}\left\{\left[-{\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right]\frac{x}{r}+(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\left(-{\rm i}\hbar\frac{z}{r}\right)\right\}-a_{3}\left(-{\rm i}\hbar S_{y}\frac{{x}}{r}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{2}\left\{\left[{\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right]\frac{x}{r}+(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\left({\rm i}\hbar\frac{z}{r}\right)\right\}+a_{3}\left({\rm i}\hbar S_{y}\frac{{x}}{r}\right).

Based on the results of (ii) and (iii), we have known that

\displaystyle[\ell_{x},K_{y}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{2}\left\{\left[-{\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{{x}}\right]\frac{y}{r}+(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r}){\rm i}\hbar\frac{z}{r}\right\}+a_{3}\left[-{\rm i}\hbar S_{x}\frac{y}{r}\right],

(302)

\displaystyle[K_{x},\ell_{y}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{2}\left\{\left[{\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right]\frac{x}{r}+(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\left({\rm i}\hbar\frac{z}{r}\right)\right\}+a_{3}\left({\rm i}\hbar S_{y}\frac{{x}}{r}\right),

(303)

which lead to

\displaystyle[\ell_{x},K_{y}]+[K_{x},\ell_{y}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{2}\left\{\left[-{\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x}\right]\frac{y}{r}+(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r}){\rm i}\hbar\frac{z}{r}\right\}-a_{3}{\rm i}\hbar S_{x}\frac{y}{r}+a_{2}\left\{\left[{\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right]\frac{x}{r}+(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r}){\rm i}\hbar\frac{z}{r}\right\}+a_{3}{\rm i}\hbar S_{y}\frac{x}{r}

(304)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar a_{2}\left[\frac{x}{r}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}-\frac{y}{r}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x}+2(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{z}{r}\right]+{\rm i}\hbar a_{3}\left(\frac{x}{r}S_{y}-\frac{y}{r}S_{x}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar a_{2}\left[(\hat{r}\times(\hat{r}\times\vec{S}))_{z}+2(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{z}{r}\right]+{\rm i}\hbar a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar a_{2}\left[(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{z}{r}-S_{z}+2(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{z}{r}\right]+{\rm i}\hbar a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar\left[-a_{2}S_{z}+3a_{2}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{z}{r}+a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}\right].

(iv) For the fourth term, we can have

\displaystyle[K_{x},K_{y}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[a_{1}S_{x}+a_{2}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{x}{r}+a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x},a_{1}S_{y}+a_{2}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{y}{r}+a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right].

(305)

Expanding this commutator gives nine terms. We can calculate each term according to the coefficient.

(iv-1) For the
a_{1}^{2}
term, we can have

\displaystyle a_{1}^{2}[S_{x},S_{y}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar a_{1}^{2}S_{z}.

(306)

(iv-2) For the cross terms between
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
, because

\displaystyle[S_{x},\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r}]={\rm i}\hbar\frac{y}{r}S_{z}-{\rm i}\hbar\frac{z}{r}S_{y}={\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x},

(307)

\displaystyle[\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r},S_{y}]={\rm i}\hbar\frac{x}{r}S_{z}-{\rm i}\hbar\frac{z}{r}S_{x}=-{\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y},

(308)

we can have

\displaystyle a_{1}a_{2}\left[S_{x},(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{y}{r}\right]+a_{1}a_{2}\left[(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{x}{r},S_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}a_{2}\left[S_{x},\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r}\right]\frac{y}{r}+a_{1}a_{2}\left[\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r},S_{y}\right]\frac{x}{r}

(309)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}a_{2}\left[{\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x}\right]\frac{y}{r}-a_{1}a_{2}\left[{\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right]\frac{x}{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar a_{1}a_{2}\left[\frac{x}{r}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}-\frac{y}{r}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar a_{1}a_{2}\left[\hat{r}\times(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})\right]_{z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar a_{1}a_{2}\left[(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{z}{r}-S_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar a_{1}a_{2}\left[S_{z}-(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{z}{r}\right].

(iv-3) For the cross terms between
a_{1}
and
a_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle a_{1}a_{3}\left[S_{x},(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right]+a_{1}a_{3}\left[(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x},S_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}a_{3}\left[S_{x},\frac{z}{r}S_{x}-\frac{x}{r}S_{z}\right]+a_{1}a_{3}\left[\frac{y}{r}S_{z}-\frac{z}{r}S_{y},S_{y}\right]

(310)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}a_{3}\left(-\frac{x}{r}[S_{x},S_{z}]\right)+a_{1}a_{3}\left(\frac{y}{r}[S_{z},S_{y}]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}a_{3}\left({\rm i}\hbar S_{y}\frac{x}{r}\right)+a_{1}a_{3}\left(-{\rm i}\hbar S_{x}\frac{y}{r}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar a_{1}a_{3}\left(\frac{x}{r}S_{y}-\frac{y}{r}S_{x}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar a_{1}a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}.

(iv-4) For the
a_{2}^{2}
term, we have

\displaystyle a_{2}^{2}\left[(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{x}{r},(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{y}{r}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

(311)

(iv-5) For the cross terms between
a_{2}
and
a_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle a_{2}a_{3}\left[(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{x}{r},(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right]+a_{2}a_{3}\left[(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x},(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{y}{r}\right]=a_{2}a_{3}\frac{x}{r}\left[\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r},(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right]-a_{2}a_{3}\frac{y}{r}\left[\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r},(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x}\right].

(312)

Because

\displaystyle[\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r},S_{x}]=-{\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x},

(313)

\displaystyle[\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r},S_{z}]=-{\rm i}\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z},

(314)

we have

\displaystyle\left[\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r},(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r},\frac{z}{r}S_{x}-\frac{x}{r}S_{z}\right]=-{\rm i}\hbar\left[\frac{z}{r}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x}-\frac{x}{r}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}\right],

(315)

and similarly,

\displaystyle\left[\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r},(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar\left[\frac{y}{r}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}-\frac{z}{r}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right].

(316)

Substituting these back, we can have

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar a_{2}a_{3}\left\{\frac{x}{r}\left[\frac{z}{r}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x}-\frac{x}{r}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}\right]-\frac{y}{r}\left[\frac{y}{r}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}-\frac{z}{r}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right]\right\}

(317)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar a_{2}a_{3}\left[\frac{z}{r}\left(\frac{x}{r}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x}+\frac{y}{r}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right)-\frac{x^{2}+y^{2}}{r^{2}}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar a_{2}a_{3}\left[-\frac{z^{2}}{r^{2}}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}-\frac{x^{2}+y^{2}}{r^{2}}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar a_{2}a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}.

(iv-6) For the
a_{3}^{2}
term, we have

\displaystyle a_{3}^{2}\left[(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{x},(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{3}^{2}\left[\frac{y}{r}S_{z}-\frac{z}{r}S_{y},\frac{z}{r}S_{x}-\frac{x}{r}S_{z}\right]

(318)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{3}^{2}\left(\frac{yz}{r^{2}}[S_{z},S_{x}]+\frac{zx}{r^{2}}[S_{y},S_{z}]-\frac{z^{2}}{r^{2}}[S_{y},S_{x}]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar a_{3}^{2}\left(\frac{yz}{r^{2}}S_{y}+\frac{zx}{r^{2}}S_{x}+\frac{z^{2}}{r^{2}}S_{z}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar a_{3}^{2}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{z}{r}.

After adding all of these terms together, we can have

\displaystyle[K_{x},K_{y}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar\left[a_{1}^{2}S_{z}+a_{1}a_{2}\left(S_{z}-(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{z}{r}\right)+a_{1}a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}+a_{2}a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}+a_{3}^{2}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{z}{r}\right]

(319)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar\left[(a_{1}^{2}+a_{1}a_{2})S_{z}+(a_{3}^{2}-a_{1}a_{2})(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{z}{r}+(a_{1}a_{3}+a_{2}a_{3})(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}\right].

Finally, we can calculate
[L_{x},L_{y}]
as

\displaystyle[L_{x},L_{y}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\ell_{x},\ell_{y}]+[\ell_{x},K_{y}]+[K_{x},\ell_{y}]+[K_{x},K_{y}]

(320)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar\ell_{z}+{\rm i}\hbar\left[-a_{2}S_{z}+3a_{2}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{z}{r}+a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}\right]

\displaystyle+{\rm i}\hbar\left[(a_{1}^{2}+a_{1}a_{2})S_{z}+(a_{3}^{2}-a_{1}a_{2})(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{z}{r}+(a_{1}a_{3}+a_{2}a_{3})(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar\left\{\ell_{z}+(a_{1}^{2}+a_{1}a_{2}-a_{2})S_{z}+(3a_{2}+a_{3}^{2}-a_{1}a_{2})(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{z}{r}+(a_{3}+a_{1}a_{3}+a_{2}a_{3})(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}\right\}.

What we need is
[L_{x},L_{y}]={\rm i}\hbar L_{z}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle[L_{x},L_{y}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar\left[\ell_{z}+a_{1}S_{z}+a_{2}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\frac{z}{r}+a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})_{z}\right].

(321)

After comparing the coefficients of the corresponding terms, we obtain the following equations for
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}
:

\displaystyle a_{1}^{2}+a_{1}a_{2}-a_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1},

\displaystyle 3a_{2}+a_{3}^{2}-a_{1}a_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{2},

\displaystyle a_{3}+a_{1}a_{3}+a_{2}a_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{3}.

(322)

Analysis 1.—
From the third of Eq. (
24
), we have

\displaystyle a_{3}(a_{1}+a_{2})=0.

(323)

If
a_{1}+a_{2}\neq 0
, we must have
a_{3}=0
, the remaining equations give
a_{1}=1
and
a_{2}=0
, which corresponds to the simple “displacement” result
\vec{L}=\vec{\ell}+\vec{S}
.

If
a_{1}+a_{2}=0
, the equations yield
a_{2}=-a_{1}
, and

\displaystyle-2a_{1}+a_{3}^{2}+a_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

(324)

i.e.,

\displaystyle(a_{1}-1)^{2}+a_{3}^{2}=1.

(325)

If the coefficients
a_{2}
and
a_{3}
are real numbers, then Eq. (
325
) describes a continuous family of solutions parameterized by
\theta
. One can parameterize them as

\displaystyle a_{1}-1=-\cos 2\theta,

\displaystyle a_{3}=-\sin 2\theta.

(326)

(Note: since
\theta
is arbitrary, one can parameterize
a_{3}
as either
a_{3}=-\sin 2\theta
or
a_{3}=\sin 2\theta
). We find that

\displaystyle a_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sin^{2}\theta,

\displaystyle a_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sin^{2}\theta,

\displaystyle a_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sin\theta\cos\theta,

(327)

which just comes from the “rotation” solution

\displaystyle\vec{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}-2\sin\theta\cos\theta\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r}+2\sin^{2}\theta\left[\vec{S}-\frac{(\vec{S}\cdot\vec{r})\vec{r}}{r^{2}}\right].

(328)

Analysis 2.—
Note that the derivation in this
Remark
only depends on the commutation relations of the angular momentum operator (i.e.,
\vec{S}\times\vec{S}={\rm i}\hbar\vec{S}
), without using the special spin-1/2 condition (i.e.,
(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})^{2}=\openone
), thus this result is valid for any spin-
s
angular momentum operator.
In summary, for arbitrary spin-
s
angular momentum, the solution of the function
\vec{L}=F(\vec{\ell},\vec{S})
is given by

\vec{L}=F(\vec{\ell},\vec{S})=\begin{cases}\vec{\ell}+\vec{S},\\
\vec{\ell}-2\sin\theta\cos\theta\dfrac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r}+2\sin^{2}\theta\left[\vec{S}-\dfrac{(\vec{S}\cdot\vec{r})\vec{r}}{r^{2}}\right].\end{cases}

(329)

However, for simplicity, in this work, we restrict our study on the spin-1/2 case.

Analysis 3.—
Usually, the angular momentum operator
\vec{L}=(L_{x},L_{y},L_{z})
is a hermitian operator, i.e.,

\displaystyle\vec{L}=(\vec{L})^{\dagger},

(330)

or equivalently

\displaystyle L_{j}=L_{j}^{\dagger},\;\;\;\;\;\;(j=x,y,z).

(331)

If one relaxes the hermitian condition, but only keeps the following angular-momentum condition

\displaystyle\vec{L}\times\vec{L}={\rm i}\hbar\vec{L},

(332)

then one can have other solutions of the complex
\vec{L}
with complex coefficients
a_{1}
,
a_{2}
, and
a_{3}
. Due to the following identity of hyperbolic function

\displaystyle\cosh^{2}\vartheta-\sinh^{2}\vartheta=1,

(333)

(i) one can parameterize
a_{1}
and
a_{3}
as

\displaystyle(a_{1}-1)^{2}=\cosh^{2}\vartheta,\;\;\;\;\ a_{3}^{2}=-\sinh^{2}\vartheta,

(334)

i.e.,

\displaystyle a_{1}-1=\pm\cosh\vartheta,\;\;\;\;\ a_{3}={\rm i}\,\sinh\vartheta,

(335)

i.e.,

\displaystyle a_{1}=1\pm\cosh\vartheta,\;\;\;\;\ a_{3}={\rm i}\,\sinh\vartheta.

(336)

(ii) Alternatively, one can parameterize
a_{2}
and
a_{3}
as

\displaystyle a_{3}^{2}=\cosh^{2}\vartheta,\;\;\;\;\ (a_{1}-1)^{2}=-\sinh^{2}\vartheta,

(337)

i.e.,

\displaystyle a_{3}=\pm\cosh\vartheta,\;\;\;\;\ a_{1}=1+{\rm i}\sinh^{2}\vartheta.

(338)

\blacksquare

V.4.2
Extracting General Spin Vector Potential from
\vec{L}=F(\vec{\ell},\vec{S})

From previous section, we have known that the general solutions of the angular momentum operator
\vec{L}=F(\vec{\ell},\vec{S})
are given by

\vec{L}=F(\vec{\ell},\vec{S})=\vec{\ell}+a_{1}\vec{S}+a_{2}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}+a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S}),

(339)

with
solution 1
as
\{a_{1}=1,a_{2}=0,a_{3}=0\}
and
solution 2
as
\{a_{2}=-a_{1},(a_{1}-1)^{2}+a_{3}^{2}=1\}
. Let us denote

\displaystyle\vec{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}+q\vec{G},

(340)

with

\displaystyle q\vec{G}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}\vec{S}+a_{2}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}+a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S}).

(341)

Then based on the
vector potential extraction approach
, we can extract the spin vector potential as

\displaystyle\vec{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c\dfrac{\vec{r}\times\vec{G}}{r^{2}}=\frac{c}{q}\dfrac{\hat{r}\times\left[a_{1}\vec{S}+a_{2}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}+a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})\right]}{r}

(342)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{c}{q}\dfrac{\hat{r}\times\left[a_{1}\vec{S}+a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})\right]}{r}=a_{1}\frac{c}{q}\dfrac{\hat{r}\times\vec{S}}{r}+a_{3}\frac{c}{q}\dfrac{\hat{r}\times(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})}{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}\frac{c}{q}\dfrac{\hat{r}\times\vec{S}}{r}+a_{3}\frac{c}{q}\dfrac{(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}-\vec{S}}{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\frac{c}{q}\left[a_{1}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})-a_{3}\vec{S}+a_{3}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}\right].

Remark 25
.

Specially, for
a_{1}=1
and
a_{3}=0
, we can recover the previous spin vector potential in Eq. (
259
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\vec{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c\dfrac{\vec{r}\times\vec{G}}{r^{2}}=\frac{c}{q}\dfrac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r^{2}}.

(343)

\blacksquare

Remark 26
.

In the spherical coordinate system, the spin vector potential can be written as

\displaystyle\vec{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{r}\hat{e}_{r}+A_{\theta}\hat{e}_{\theta}+A_{\phi}\hat{e}_{\phi}.

(344)

One can check that
A_{r}=0
. Explicitly, one has

\displaystyle A_{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{A}\cdot\hat{e}_{r}=\frac{1}{r}\frac{c}{q}\left[a_{1}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})-a_{3}\vec{S}+a_{3}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}\right]\cdot\hat{e}_{r}

(345)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\frac{c}{q}\left[a_{1}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})-a_{3}\vec{S}+a_{3}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}\right]\cdot\hat{r}=\frac{1}{r}\frac{c}{q}\left[-a_{3}\vec{S}+a_{3}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}\right]\cdot\hat{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\frac{c}{q}a_{3}\left[-\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r}+(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\right]=0.

\blacksquare

V.4.3
Discussion

In the previous subsection, we have studied the spin vector potential from the viewpoint of angular momentum operator. To deepen understanding this topic, in this subsection, let us study the spin vector potential from the viewpoint of linear momentum operator. As before, we restrict our discussion on spin-1/2 case.

Discussion 1: The Gauge Transformation of the Momentum.—
Now, we take the unitary transformation
\mathcal{U}={\rm e}^{{\rm i}\theta\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}}
as the gauge transformation, and we apply it to the linear momentum
\vec{p}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\vec{p}\mapsto\vec{\Pi}=\mathcal{U}\,\vec{p}\,\mathcal{U}^{\dagger},

(346)

here
\vec{\Pi}
plays a role of canonical momentum. Because

\displaystyle(\mathcal{U}\,\vec{p}\,\mathcal{U}^{\dagger})\psi=\mathcal{U}(-{\rm i}\hbar\vec{\nabla})(\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}\psi)=-{\rm i}\hbar\,\mathcal{U}\left[(\vec{\nabla}\mathcal{U}^{\dagger})\psi+\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}\vec{\nabla}\psi\right]=-{\rm i}\hbar\,\mathcal{U}(\vec{\nabla}\mathcal{U}^{\dagger})\psi-{\rm i}\hbar\vec{\nabla}\psi,

(347)

\displaystyle\vec{\nabla}(\mathcal{U}\mathcal{U}^{\dagger})=(\vec{\nabla}\mathcal{U})\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}+\mathcal{U}(\vec{\nabla}\mathcal{U}^{\dagger})=0,

(348)

we can have

\displaystyle(\mathcal{U}\,\vec{p}\,\mathcal{U}^{\dagger})\psi={\rm i}\hbar(\vec{\nabla}\mathcal{U})\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}\psi-{\rm i}\hbar\vec{\nabla}\psi=\left[\vec{p}+{\rm i}\hbar(\vec{\nabla}\mathcal{U})\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}\right]\psi,

(349)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\vec{p}\mapsto\vec{\Pi}=\mathcal{U}\,\vec{p}\,\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}=\vec{p}+{\rm i}\hbar(\vec{\nabla}\mathcal{U})\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}.

(350)

Because

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}={\rm e}^{{\rm i}\theta\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}}=\cos\theta\openone+{\rm i}\sin\theta(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}={\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\theta\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}}=\cos\theta\openone-{\rm i}\sin\theta(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}),

(351)

we have

\displaystyle\partial_{i}\mathcal{U}={\rm i}\sin\theta\,\partial_{i}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})=\frac{{\rm i}\sin\theta}{r}\left(\Gamma_{i}-(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}_{i}\right),\quad\quad(i=x,y,z),

(352)

therefore

\displaystyle(\partial_{i}\mathcal{U})\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm i}\sin\theta}{r}\left(\Gamma_{i}-(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}_{i}\right)\left[\cos\theta-{\rm i}\sin\theta(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\right]

(353)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm i}}{r}\left[\sin\theta\cos\theta\left(\Gamma_{i}-(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}_{i}\right)-{\rm i}\,{\sin^{2}\theta}\left(\hat{r}_{i}{+}{\rm i}(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})_{i}\right)+{\rm i}\,{\sin^{2}\theta}\hat{r}_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm i}}{r}\left[\sin\theta\cos\theta\left(\Gamma_{i}-(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}_{i}\right)+\sin^{2}\theta(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})_{i}\right],

where we used

\displaystyle\Gamma_{z}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{z}(\Gamma_{x}x+\Gamma_{y}y+\Gamma_{z}z)\frac{1}{r}=({\rm i}\Gamma_{y}x-{\rm i}\Gamma_{x}y+z)\frac{1}{r}

(354)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{z}{r}+{\rm i}(x\Gamma_{y}-y\Gamma_{x})\frac{1}{r}=\frac{z}{r}+{\rm i}(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})_{z},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{i}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})=\hat{r}_{i}{+}{\rm i}(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})_{i},\;\;\;\;\;(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})^{2}=\openone.

(355)

Then one has

\displaystyle(\vec{\nabla}\mathcal{U})\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm i}}{r}\left[\sin\theta\cos\theta\left(\vec{\Gamma}-(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}\right)+\sin^{2}\theta(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})\right]

(356)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm i}}{r}\left[\sin^{2}\theta(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})+\sin\theta\cos\theta\left(\vec{\Gamma}-(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}\right)\right],

thus

\displaystyle\vec{p}\mapsto\vec{\Pi}=\mathcal{U}\,\vec{p}\,\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{p}+{\rm i}\hbar(\vec{\nabla}\mathcal{U})\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}

(357)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{p}-\frac{\hbar}{r}\left[\sin^{2}\theta(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})+\sin\theta\cos\theta\left(\vec{\Gamma}-(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}\right)\right].

Discussion 2: Getting the Vector Potential from the Gauge Transformation.—
We’d like to verify the unification of linear momentum method and angular momentum method. Notice that

\displaystyle\hat{r}\times\left[\sin^{2}\theta(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})+\sin\theta\cos\theta\left(\vec{\Gamma}-(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}\right)\right]=\left[\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})+\sin^{2}\theta\left((\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}-\vec{\Gamma}\right)\right].

(358)

We can have

\displaystyle\vec{L}=\mathcal{U}\,\vec{\ell}\,\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}=\hat{r}\times\mathcal{U}\,\vec{p}\,\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}=\hat{r}\times\vec{p}-\frac{\hbar}{r}\left[\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})+\sin^{2}\theta\left((\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}-\vec{\Gamma}\right)\right],

(359)

which is the same as Eq. (
276
). Hence, the two methods are unified.

Now, we use minimal coupling to find the potential lying under the gauge transformation. Remember that

\displaystyle\vec{\Pi}=\vec{p}-\frac{g}{c}\vec{A}.

(360)

Comparing with

\displaystyle\vec{\Pi}=\mathcal{U}\,\vec{p}\,\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}=\vec{p}+{\rm i}\hbar(\vec{\nabla}\mathcal{U})\mathcal{U}^{\dagger},

(361)

we can have

\displaystyle A_{i}=-{\rm i}\frac{c\hbar}{g}(\partial_{i}\mathcal{U})\mathcal{U}^{\dagger},\quad\quad(i=1,2,3).

(362)

Because

\displaystyle(\partial_{i}\mathcal{U})\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}=\frac{{\rm i}}{r}\left[\sin^{2}\theta(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})_{i}+\sin\theta\cos\theta\left(\Gamma_{i}-(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}_{i}\right)\right],

(363)

we finally find

\displaystyle\vec{A}={\rm i}\frac{c}{g}(\vec{\nabla}\mathcal{U})\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}=\frac{c}{g}\frac{\hbar}{r}\left[\sin^{2}\theta(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})+\sin\theta\cos\theta\left(\vec{\Gamma}-(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}\right)\right].

(364)

Remark 27
.

We illustrate the central role of gauge transformations of the field. Under a general gauge transformation, we have obtained

\displaystyle A_{\mu}^{\prime}=\mathcal{U}A_{\mu}\mathcal{U}^{-1}+{\rm i}\frac{c\hbar}{g}(\partial_{\mu}\mathcal{U})\mathcal{U}^{-1}.

(365)

Now, taking
A_{\mu}=0
to adapt to the case of a completely absent gauge potential, we have

\displaystyle A_{\mu}^{\prime}={\rm i}\frac{c\hbar}{g}(\partial_{\mu}\mathcal{U})\mathcal{U}^{-1},\quad(i=1,2,3).

(366)

Note that

\displaystyle A_{\mu}=(\varphi,-\vec{A}),

(367)

\displaystyle A_{i}^{\prime}=-{\rm i}\frac{c\hbar}{g}(\partial_{i}\mathcal{U})\mathcal{U}^{-1},\;\;\;\;\;(\mu=0,1,2,3;\;i=1,2,3).

(368)

This is precisely the result obtained from linear momentum method (In this case, the gauge transformation does not involve time).
\blacksquare

Remark 28
.

We find that a symmetric algebra is at work here:

\displaystyle\hat{r}\times\left[\sin^{2}\theta(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})+\sin\theta\cos\theta\left(\vec{\Gamma}-(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}\right)\right]=\left[\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})+\sin^{2}\theta\left((\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}-\vec{\Gamma}\right)\right],

(369)

\displaystyle\hat{r}\times\left[\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})+\sin^{2}\theta\left((\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}-\vec{\Gamma}\right)\right]=-\left[\sin^{2}\theta(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})+\sin\theta\cos\theta\left(\vec{\Gamma}-(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}\right)\right].

(370)

That is to say, the linear momentum operator and the angular momentum operator exhibit a certain dual structure after the gauge transformation.
\blacksquare

VI
General Static Solutions for the SU(2) Yang-Mills Theory

In previous section, we have determined the general angular momentum operator
\vec{L}=F(\vec{\ell},\vec{S})
as

\vec{L}=F(\vec{\ell},\vec{S})=\vec{\ell}+a_{1}\vec{S}+a_{2}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}+a_{3}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S}),

(371)

where
solution 1
is
\{a_{1}=1,a_{2}=0,a_{3}=0\}
, which comes from the simple “displacement”, and
solution 2
is
\{a_{2}=-a_{1},(a_{1}-1)^{2}+a_{3}^{2}=1\}
, which comes from the “rotation” (i.e., the unitary transformation, or the gauge transformation). Accordingly, based on the
vector potential extraction approach
, one can extract the spin vector potential as

\displaystyle\vec{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\frac{c}{q}\left[a_{1}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})-a_{3}\vec{S}+a_{3}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}\right].

(372)

Note that the term
a_{2}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}
has no contribution to the vector potential, thus
\{a_{1}=1,a_{3}=0\}
corresponds to the vector potential of
solution 1
, and
\{a_{2}=-a_{1},(a_{1}-1)^{2}+a_{3}^{2}=1\}
corresponds to vector potential of
the solution 2
.

Remark 29
.

The spin vector potential of
solution 1
is given by

\displaystyle\vec{A}^{(1)}\equiv\vec{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{k}\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r^{2}},

(373)

with
\tilde{k}=\frac{c}{g}
(and we denote
q=g
). In Ref.
Zhou2025
, by setting the vector potential and the scalar potential as

\displaystyle\vec{A}=\tilde{k}\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r^{2}},\;\;\;\;\varphi=\tilde{f}_{1}(r)\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\tilde{f}_{2}(r),

(374)

and substitute them into the Yang-Mills equations, one can have the solutions listed in Table
1
.

Table 1:
Summary of the consistent solutions for
\tilde{f}_{1}(r)
and
\tilde{f}_{2}(r)
. Note that for the case
\tilde{g}\neq 0
,
\tilde{k}\neq 0
, solutions exist only when
\tilde{g}\hbar\tilde{k}=1
or
2
.

(i)
\tilde{g}=0
,
\tilde{k}=0

(ii)
\tilde{g}=0
,
\tilde{k}\neq 0

(iii)
\tilde{g}\neq 0
,
\tilde{k}=0

(iv)
\tilde{g}\neq 0
,
\tilde{k}\neq 0

\tilde{f}_{1}(r)=\dfrac{\kappa_{2}}{r^{3}}+\kappa_{3}

\tilde{f}_{2}(r)=\dfrac{\kappa_{1}}{r}

No solutions

\tilde{f}_{1}(r)=0

\tilde{f}_{2}(r)=\dfrac{\kappa_{1}}{r}

If
\tilde{g}\hbar\tilde{k}=2
:
\tilde{f}_{1}(r)=0

If
\tilde{g}\hbar\tilde{k}=1
:
\tilde{f}_{1}(r)=\dfrac{\kappa_{2}}{r}+\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r^{2}}

\tilde{f}_{2}(r)=\dfrac{\kappa_{1}}{r}

Note that in the subsequent calculations, for convenience, we shall use uniformly the notation of vector potential and scalar potential as

\displaystyle\vec{A}=\frac{1}{r}\left[k_{1}(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})\right],\;\;\;\;\varphi=f_{1}(r)\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)+f_{2}(r),

(375)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\{\vec{S},\vec{r}\}\mapsto\{\vec{\Gamma},\hat{r}\},\;\;\;\;\vec{S}=\frac{\hbar}{2}\,\vec{\Gamma},\;\;\;\;\hat{r}\equiv\hat{e}_{r}=\frac{\vec{r}}{r},

(376)

thus

\displaystyle\tilde{k}\frac{\hbar}{2}\mapsto k_{1},\;\;\;\;r\tilde{f}_{1}(r)\frac{\hbar}{2}\mapsto\tilde{f}_{1}(r),\;\;\;\;\tilde{f}_{2}(r)\mapsto f_{2}(r).

(377)

Therefore, in terms of the language of
\{\vec{\Gamma},\hat{r}\}
, in Table
1
, the solution of
f_{1}(r)
will be multiplied by
r
, and the solution of
f_{2}(r)
is unchanged. The merit of using
\{\vec{\Gamma},\hat{r}\}
is that
\{\vec{\Gamma},\hat{r}\}
are dimensionless.
\blacksquare

Remark 30
.

The spin vector potential of
solution 2
is given by

\displaystyle\vec{A}^{(2)}\equiv\vec{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\frac{c}{q}\left[a_{1}(\hat{r}\times\vec{S})-a_{3}\vec{S}+a_{3}(\vec{S}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}\right].

(378)

When
a_{i}
’s are real numbers, they can be parameterized as

\displaystyle a_{1}=2\sin^{2}\theta,\;\;\;\;\;a_{3}=-2\sin\theta\cos\theta.

(379)

Here
\vec{A}
is a pure gauge solution for the Yang-Mills equations. A pure gauge solution means that the field strength tensor
F_{\mu\nu}
vanishes identically, i.e., the gauge potential can be expressed as

A_{\mu}=-{\rm i}\frac{c\hbar}{g}(\partial_{i}\mathcal{U})\mathcal{U}^{-1}

for some gauge transformation
\mathcal{U}
. In the present case, we consider a static pure gauge with
\varphi=0
.
\blacksquare

Remark 31
.

The spin vector potentials of
solution 1
and
solution 2
can be rewritten in the following form

\displaystyle\vec{A}=\frac{1}{r}\left[k_{1}(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})+k_{2}\vec{\Gamma}+k_{3}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}\right],

(380)

with

\displaystyle\vec{S}=\frac{\hbar}{2}\,\vec{\Gamma},\;\;\;\;\hat{r}\equiv\hat{e}_{r}=\frac{\vec{r}}{r},

\displaystyle k_{1}={\frac{c\hbar}{2q}}a_{1},\;\;\;\;k_{2}=-\frac{c\hbar}{2q}a_{3},\;\;\;\;k_{3}=\frac{c\hbar}{2q}a_{3},

(381)

and the constraint

\displaystyle k_{2}+k_{3}=0.

(382)

In this work, we restrict our study on the spin-1/2 case, then
\vec{\Gamma}
is the vector of Pauli’s matrices. In the following, by setting the scalar potential as

\displaystyle\varphi=f_{1}(r)\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)+f_{2}(r),

(383)

we shall solve the general static solutions for the SU(2) Yang-Mills equations. Here, for convenience of calculation, we have used
\vec{\Gamma}
to replace
\vec{S}
, and also we shall regard the parameter
q
as the parameter
g
.
\blacksquare

VI.1
Calculation of the Magnetic-like Field and the Electric-like Field

In this section, let us calculate the magnetic-like field and the electric-like field, i.e.,

\displaystyle\vec{{B}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\nabla}\times\vec{{A}}-\frac{{\rm i}g}{c\hbar}\left(\vec{{A}}\times\vec{{A}}\right),

\displaystyle\vec{{E}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{c}\dfrac{\partial\,\vec{{A}}}{\partial\,t}-\vec{\nabla}\varphi-\frac{{\rm i}g}{c\hbar}\left[\varphi,\ \vec{{A}}\right].

(384)

Remark 32
.

Calculation of the Magnetic-like Field.

Due to symmetry, we only need to calculate the
z
-component of
\vec{B}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle B_{z}=\partial_{x}A_{y}-\partial_{y}A_{x}-{\rm i}\frac{g}{\hbar c}[A_{x},A_{y}].

(385)

We have

\displaystyle A_{x}=\frac{1}{r}\left[k_{1}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right)+k_{2}\Gamma_{x}+k_{3}\hat{r}_{x}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\right],

(386)

\displaystyle A_{y}=\frac{1}{r}\left[k_{1}\left(\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right)+k_{2}\Gamma_{y}+k_{3}\hat{r}_{y}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\right],

(387)

with the simple notations

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}=\frac{x}{r},\;\;\;\;\hat{r}_{y}=\frac{y}{r},\;\;\;\;\hat{r}_{z}=\frac{z}{r}.

(388)

(i)
The Curl Part.—
Based on

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)}{\partial x}=-\frac{x}{r^{3}}=-\frac{\hat{r}_{x}}{r^{2}},\;\;\;\;\frac{\partial\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)}{\partial y}=-\frac{y}{r^{3}}=-\frac{\hat{r}_{y}}{r^{2}},\;\;\;\;\frac{\partial\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)}{\partial z}=-\frac{z}{r^{3}}=-\frac{\hat{r}_{z}}{r^{2}},

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\hat{r}_{x}}{\partial x}=\frac{\partial\left(\dfrac{x}{r}\right)}{\partial x}=\frac{1}{r}-x\frac{x}{r^{3}}=\frac{1}{r}-\frac{x^{2}}{r^{3}}=\frac{1}{r}-\frac{\hat{r}_{x}^{2}}{r}=\frac{1-\hat{r}_{x}^{2}}{r},\;\;\;\;\frac{\partial\hat{r}_{y}}{\partial y}=\frac{1-\hat{r}_{y}^{2}}{r},\;\;\;\;\frac{\partial\hat{r}_{z}}{\partial z}=\frac{1-\hat{r}_{z}^{2}}{r},

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\hat{r}_{y}}{\partial x}=\frac{\partial\left(\dfrac{y}{r}\right)}{\partial x}=y\frac{\partial\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)}{\partial x}=-y\frac{\hat{r}_{x}}{r^{2}}=-\frac{\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}}{r},\;\;\;\;\frac{\partial\hat{r}_{z}}{\partial x}=-\frac{\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{z}}{r},

(389)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)}{\partial x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial(\Gamma_{x}\hat{r}_{x}+\Gamma_{y}\hat{r}_{y}+\Gamma_{z}\hat{r}_{z})}{\partial x}=\Gamma_{x}\frac{\partial\hat{r}_{x}}{\partial x}+\Gamma_{y}\frac{\partial\hat{r}_{y}}{\partial x}+\Gamma_{z}\frac{\partial\hat{r}_{z}}{\partial x}

(390)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{x}\frac{1-\hat{r}_{x}^{2}}{r}+\Gamma_{y}\left(-\frac{\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}}{r}\right)+\Gamma_{z}\left(-\frac{\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{z}}{r}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{x}\frac{1-\hat{r}_{x}^{2}}{r}-\Gamma_{y}\left(\frac{\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}}{r}\right)-\Gamma_{z}\left(-\frac{\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{z}}{r}\right)=\frac{1}{r}\Gamma_{x}-\frac{\hat{r}_{x}}{r}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}),

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)}{\partial y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\Gamma_{y}-\frac{\hat{r}_{y}}{r}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}),

(391)

we have

\displaystyle\partial_{x}A_{y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\left\{\frac{1}{r}\left[k_{1}\left(\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right)+k_{2}\Gamma_{y}+k_{3}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]\right\}

(392)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\hat{r}_{x}}{r^{2}}\left[k_{1}\left(\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right)+k_{2}\Gamma_{y}+k_{3}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{r}\left[-k_{1}\frac{\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{z}}{r}\Gamma_{x}-k_{1}\frac{1-\hat{r}_{x}^{2}}{r}\Gamma_{z}+k_{3}\left(-\frac{\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}}{r}\right)\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)+k_{3}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\frac{1}{r}\Gamma_{x}-\frac{\hat{r}_{x}}{r}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\hat{r}_{x}}{r^{2}}\left[k_{1}\left(\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right)+k_{2}\Gamma_{y}+k_{3}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{r}\left[-k_{1}\frac{\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{z}}{r}\Gamma_{x}-k_{1}\frac{1-\hat{r}_{x}^{2}}{r}\Gamma_{z}+\frac{k_{3}}{r}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}-2\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left\{k_{1}\left[2\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}+\left(1-2\hat{r}_{x}^{2}\right)\Gamma_{z}\right]+k_{2}\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-k_{3}\left[\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}-3\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]\right\},

and

\displaystyle\partial_{y}A_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\left\{\frac{1}{r}\left[k_{1}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right)+k_{2}\Gamma_{x}+k_{3}\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]\right\}

(393)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\hat{r}_{y}}{r^{2}}\left[k_{1}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right)+k_{2}\Gamma_{x}+k_{3}\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\left[k_{1}\frac{1-\hat{r}_{y}^{2}}{r}\Gamma_{z}+k_{1}\frac{\hat{r}_{y}\hat{r}_{z}}{r}\Gamma_{y}+\frac{k_{3}}{r}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-2\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left\{-k_{1}\left[\left(1-2\hat{r}_{y}^{2}\right)\Gamma_{z}+2\hat{r}_{y}\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right]+k_{2}\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}-k_{3}\left[\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-3\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]\right\}.

So we can have

\displaystyle\partial_{x}A_{y}-\partial_{y}A_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left\{k_{1}\left[2\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}+\Gamma_{z}-2\hat{r}_{x}^{2}\Gamma_{z}\right]+k_{2}\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-k_{3}\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}+3k_{3}\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right\}

(394)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left\{-k_{1}\left[\Gamma_{z}-2\hat{r}_{y}^{2}\Gamma_{z}+2\hat{r}_{y}\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right]+k_{2}\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}-k_{3}\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}+3k_{3}\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\biggr\{k_{1}\left[2\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}+2\hat{r}_{y}\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}+2\Gamma_{z}-2\left(\hat{r}_{x}^{2}+\hat{r}_{y}^{2}\right)\Gamma_{z}\right]

\displaystyle+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}+\left[3k_{3}\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)-3k_{3}\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]\biggr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left\{2k_{1}\left[\hat{r}_{z}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}\right)+\Gamma_{z}\left(1-\hat{r}_{x}^{2}-\hat{r}_{y}^{2}\right)\right]+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left\{2k_{1}\left[\hat{r}_{z}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}\right)+\Gamma_{z}\hat{r}_{z}^{2}\right]+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}\right)_{z}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[2k_{1}\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}\right)_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[2k_{1}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{z}+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}\right)_{z}\right].

(ii)
The Commutator Part.—
We have

\displaystyle[A_{x},A_{y}]=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[k_{1}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right)+k_{2}\Gamma_{x}+k_{3}\hat{r}_{x}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}),\;k_{1}\left(\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right)+k_{2}\Gamma_{y}+k_{3}\hat{r}_{y}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\right].

(395)

We calculate the nine commutators as follows.

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y},\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{y}\hat{r}_{z}\left[\Gamma_{z},\Gamma_{x}\right]-\hat{r}_{y}\hat{r}_{x}\left[\Gamma_{z},\Gamma_{z}\right]-\hat{r}_{z}^{2}\left[\Gamma_{y},\Gamma_{x}\right]+\hat{r}_{z}\hat{r}_{x}\left[\Gamma_{y},\Gamma_{z}\right]

(396)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{y}\hat{r}_{z}\left(2{\rm i}\Gamma_{y}\right)-0-\hat{r}_{z}^{2}\left(-2i\Gamma_{z}\right)+\hat{r}_{z}\hat{r}_{x}\left(2{\rm i}\Gamma_{x}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{z}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z}\right)=2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)=2{\rm i}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{z},

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y},\Gamma_{y}\right]=\hat{r}_{y}\left[\Gamma_{z},\Gamma_{y}\right]-\hat{r}_{z}\left[\Gamma_{y},\Gamma_{y}\right]=\hat{r}_{y}\left(-2{\rm i}\Gamma_{x}\right)-0=-2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x},

(397)

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y},\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{y}\left\{\hat{r}_{y}\left[\Gamma_{z},\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z}\right]-\hat{r}_{z}\left[\Gamma_{y},\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z}\right]\right\}

(398)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{y}\left\{\hat{r}_{y}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\left[2{\rm i}\Gamma_{y}\right]-\hat{r}_{y}\left[2{\rm i}\Gamma_{x}\right]\right)-\hat{r}_{z}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\left[-2{\rm i}\Gamma_{z}\right]+\hat{r}_{z}\left[2{\rm i}\Gamma_{x}\right]\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\left\{\hat{r}_{x}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z}\right)-\Gamma_{x}\left(\hat{r}_{y}^{2}+\hat{r}_{z}^{2}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\left\{\hat{r}_{x}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z}+\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}\right)-\Gamma_{x}\left(1-\hat{r}_{x}^{2}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\left[\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)-\Gamma_{x}\right],

\displaystyle\left[\Gamma_{x},\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right]=\hat{r}_{z}\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{x}\right]-\hat{r}_{x}\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{z}\right]=0-\hat{r}_{x}\left(-2{\rm i}\Gamma_{y}\right)=2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y},

(399)

\displaystyle\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{y}\right]=2{\rm i}\Gamma_{z},

(400)

\displaystyle\left[\Gamma_{x},\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]=\hat{r}_{y}\left[\Gamma_{x},\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]=\hat{r}_{y}\left[2{\rm i}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right)\right]=2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right),

(401)

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right),\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}\left\{\hat{r}_{z}\left[\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z},\Gamma_{x}\right]-\hat{r}_{x}\left[\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z},\Gamma_{z}\right]\right\}

(402)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}\left\{\hat{r}_{z}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\left[-2{\rm i}\Gamma_{z}\right]+\hat{r}_{z}\left[2{\rm i}\Gamma_{y}\right]\right)-\hat{r}_{x}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\left[-2{\rm i}\Gamma_{y}\right]+\hat{r}_{y}\left[2{\rm i}\Gamma_{x}\right]\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\left\{\hat{r}_{z}^{2}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{z}\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}+\hat{r}_{x}^{2}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\left\{\left(\hat{r}_{x}^{2}+\hat{r}_{z}^{2}\right)\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\left\{\left(1-\hat{r}_{y}^{2}\right)\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\left[\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right],

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right),\Gamma_{y}\right]=\hat{r}_{x}\left[\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right),\Gamma_{y}\right]=\hat{r}_{x}\left[-2{\rm i}\left(\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right)\right]=2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}\right),

(403)

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right),\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]=\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}\left[\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right),\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]=0.

(404)

By combining these nine terms, we can obtain

\displaystyle\left[A_{x},A_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2{\rm i}}{r^{2}}\biggr\{k_{1}^{2}\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)-k_{1}k_{2}\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}+k_{1}k_{3}\hat{r}_{y}\left[\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)-\Gamma_{x}\right]

(405)

\displaystyle+k_{2}k_{1}\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}+k_{2}^{2}\Gamma_{z}+k_{2}k_{3}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right)

\displaystyle+k_{3}k_{1}\hat{r}_{x}\left[\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]+k_{3}k_{2}\hat{r}_{x}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}\right)\biggr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2{\rm i}}{r^{2}}\biggr\{k_{1}^{2}\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)+k_{2}^{2}\Gamma_{z}+k_{1}k_{2}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}\right)

\displaystyle+k_{1}k_{3}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+k_{2}k_{3}\left[\left(\hat{r}_{x}^{2}+\hat{r}_{y}^{2}\right)\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}\right)\right]\biggr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2{\rm i}}{r^{2}}\left\{k_{1}^{2}\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)+k_{2}^{2}\Gamma_{z}+k_{1}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}\right)+k_{2}k_{3}\left[\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2{\rm i}}{r^{2}}\left[\left(k_{1}^{2}-k_{2}k_{3}\right)\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)+k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\Gamma_{z}+k_{1}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2{\rm i}}{r^{2}}\left[\left(k_{1}^{2}-k_{2}k_{3}\right)\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)+k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\Gamma_{z}+k_{1}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}\right)_{z}\right].

So we can have

\displaystyle B_{z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{x}A_{y}-\partial_{y}A_{x}-{\rm i}\frac{g}{\hbar c}\left[A_{x},A_{y}\right]

(406)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[2k_{1}\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}\right)_{z}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{2g}{\hbar c}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[\left(k_{1}^{2}-k_{2}k_{3}\right)\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)+k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\Gamma_{z}+k_{1}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}\right)_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left\{\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right]\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{z}+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)\left(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}\right)_{z}+\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\Gamma_{z}\right\}.

By symmetry, we finally have

\displaystyle\vec{B}=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left\{\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right]\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)\left(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}\right)+\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\vec{\Gamma}\right\}.

(407)

If one considers the constraint
k_{2}+k_{3}=0
, then he simply has

\displaystyle\vec{B}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left\{\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right]\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}\right\}

(408)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)+\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{3}^{2}\right]\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\frac{\vec{r}}{r^{3}}.

By considering

\displaystyle k_{1}={\frac{c\hbar}{2q}}a_{1},\;\;\;\;k_{3}=\frac{c\hbar}{2q}a_{3},\;\;\;\;q=g,

(409)

one has

\displaystyle\vec{B}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)+\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{3}^{2}\right]\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\frac{\vec{r}}{r^{3}}

(410)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[2\frac{c\hbar}{2g}a_{1}\left(\frac{a_{1}}{2}-1\right)+\frac{c\hbar}{2g}a_{3}^{2}\right]\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\frac{\vec{r}}{r^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\frac{c\hbar}{2g}a_{1}\left(a_{1}-2\right)+\frac{c\hbar}{2g}a_{3}^{2}\right]\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\frac{\vec{r}}{r^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[a_{1}\left(a_{1}-2\right)+a_{3}^{2}\right]\frac{c\hbar}{2g}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\frac{\vec{r}}{r^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[(a_{1}-1)^{2}+a_{3}^{2}-1\right]\frac{c\hbar}{2g}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\frac{\vec{r}}{r^{3}}.

Due to the constraint
(a_{1}-1)^{2}+a_{3}^{2}=1
, actually we have

\displaystyle\vec{B}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

(411)

In other words, if we do not impose the constraint condition on
k_{i}
’s
(i=1,2,3)
, then the expression of the “magnetic” field is given in
Eq. (
407
), while if we consider the constraint condition obtained by the VPEA, i.e.,

\displaystyle k_{1}={\frac{c\hbar}{2q}}a_{1},\;\;\;\;k_{2}=-\frac{c\hbar}{2q}a_{3},\;\;\;\;k_{3}=\frac{c\hbar}{2q}a_{3},

\displaystyle(a_{1}-1)^{2}+a_{3}^{2}=1,

(412)

then the “magnetic” field is zero.
\blacksquare

Remark 33
.

Calculation of the Electric-like Field.
We can have that

\displaystyle\vec{E}=-\dfrac{1}{c}\dfrac{\partial\,\vec{A}}{\partial\,t}-\vec{\nabla}\varphi-{\rm i}\,\frac{g}{\hbar c}\left[\varphi,\ \vec{A}\right]=-\vec{\nabla}\varphi-{\rm i}\,\frac{g}{\hbar c}\left[\varphi,\ \vec{A}\right]

(413)

By symmetry, it is sufficient to calculate the
x
-direction, which is

\displaystyle E_{x}=-\partial_{x}\varphi-{\rm i}\,\frac{g}{\hbar c}\left[\varphi,A_{x}\right]

(414)

(i)
The Gradient Part.—
Because

\displaystyle\varphi=f_{1}(r)(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})+f_{2}(r),

(415)

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)}{\partial x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\Gamma_{x}-\frac{\hat{r}_{x}}{r}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})={\frac{1}{r}\Gamma_{x}-\frac{x}{r^{2}}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})},

(416)

we can have

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\varphi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\left[f_{1}(r)\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)+f_{2}(r)\right]

(417)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\partial_{x}f_{1}(r)\right]\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)+f_{1}(r)\left[\partial_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]+\partial_{x}f_{2}(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{x}{r}\frac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}+f_{1}(r)\left[{\frac{1}{r}\Gamma_{x}-\frac{x}{r^{2}}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})}\right]+\frac{x}{r}\frac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{x}f_{1}(r)}{r}+\frac{x}{r}\left[\frac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\right](\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})+\frac{x}{r}\frac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{x}f_{1}(r)}{r}+\hat{r}_{x}\left[\frac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\right](\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})+\hat{r}_{x}\frac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}.

(ii)
The Commutator Part.—
We have

\displaystyle\left[\varphi,A_{x}\right]=\left[f_{1}(r)(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})+f_{2}(r),\,A_{x}\right]=f_{1}(r)\left[(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}),\,A_{x}\right].

(418)

Consider that

\displaystyle A_{x}=\frac{1}{r}\left[k_{1}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right)+k_{2}\Gamma_{x}+k_{3}\hat{r}_{x}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\right].

(419)

Note that the
k_{3}
term commutes with
A_{x}
, so we only need to calculate the following three commutators

\displaystyle\left[\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r},\;\Gamma_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{z}\right]+\hat{r}_{y}\left[\Gamma_{y},\Gamma_{z}\right]=-2{\rm i}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}\right),

\displaystyle\left[\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r},\;\Gamma_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2{\rm i}\left(\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right),

\displaystyle\left[\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r},\;\Gamma_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2{\rm i}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right).

(420)

By combining them, we get

\displaystyle\left[\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r},\;A_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2{\rm i}\frac{1}{r}\left\{k_{1}\left[\hat{r}_{y}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}\right)-\hat{r}_{z}\left(\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right)\right]+k_{2}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right)\right\}

(421)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2{\rm i}\frac{1}{r}\left\{k_{1}\left[\hat{r}_{x}\left(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{\Gamma}\right)-\Gamma_{x}\right]+k_{2}\left(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}\right)_{x}\right\}.

Based on above calculation, we can have

\displaystyle E_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial_{x}\varphi-{\rm i}\,\frac{g}{\hbar c}\left[\varphi,\;A_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[\frac{\Gamma_{x}f_{1}(r)}{r}+\hat{r}_{x}\left[\frac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\right](\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})+\hat{r}_{x}\frac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\right]-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left\{k_{1}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\left(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{\Gamma}\right)-\Gamma_{x}\right)+k_{2}\left(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}\right)_{x}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\hat{r}_{x}\frac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\left[\frac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]-\frac{2gk_{2}}{\hbar c}\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}\right)_{x}.

By symmetry, we have the electric-like field as

\displaystyle\vec{E}=-\frac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\hat{r}-\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\vec{\Gamma}-\hat{r}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\left[\frac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]-\frac{2gk_{2}}{\hbar c}\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}\right),

(423)

which does not depend on
k_{3}
.

\blacksquare

VI.2
The Constraint Equations Obtained from Solving the Yang-Mills Equations

We now come to solve the Yang-Mills equations and we shall obtain some constraint equations. We just need to solve

\displaystyle\vec{\nabla}\cdot\vec{E}\ {-}\boxed{{\rm i}\,\frac{g}{\hbar c}\Bigl(\vec{A}\cdot\vec{E}-\vec{E}\cdot\vec{A}\Bigr)}=0,

(424a)

\displaystyle\vec{\nabla}\times\vec{B}{-}\boxed{{\rm i}\,\frac{g}{\hbar c}\biggl(\Bigl[\varphi,\ \vec{E}\Bigr]+\vec{A}\times\vec{B}+\vec{B}\times\vec{A}\biggr)}=0,

(424b)

with the following quantities

\displaystyle\vec{A}=\frac{1}{r}\left[k_{1}(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})+k_{2}\vec{\Gamma}+k_{3}\hat{r}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\right],

(425)

\displaystyle\varphi=f_{1}(r)(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})+f_{2}(r),

(426)

\displaystyle\vec{B}=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left\{\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right]\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)\left(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}\right)+\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\vec{\Gamma}\right\},

(427)

\displaystyle\vec{E}=-\frac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\hat{r}-\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\vec{\Gamma}-\hat{r}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\left[\frac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]-\frac{2gk_{2}}{\hbar c}\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}\right).

(428)

VI.2.1
Checking the Equation of the Magnetic-like Field

Let us first check (
424b
). We need

\displaystyle\vec{\nabla}\times\vec{B}{-}\boxed{{\rm i}\,\frac{g}{\hbar c}\biggl(\Bigl[\varphi,\vec{E}\Bigr]+\vec{A}\times\vec{B}+\vec{B}\times\vec{A}\biggr)}=0

(429)

For the vector identity, due to symmetry, we only need to verify the
z
-component. The individual terms are calculated as follows:

(i) Consider the magnetic field commutator term:

\displaystyle\left(\vec{A}\times\vec{B}+\vec{B}\times\vec{A}\right)_{z}=\left[A_{x},B_{y}\right]-\left[A_{y},B_{x}\right].

(430)

Because

\displaystyle A_{x}=\frac{1}{r}\left[k_{1}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right)+k_{2}\Gamma_{x}+k_{3}\hat{r}_{x}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\right],

(431)

\displaystyle B_{y}=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left\{\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right]\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{y}+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)\left(\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right)+\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\Gamma_{y}\right\},

(432)

we can calculate the following nine commutators:

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y},\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\left[\hat{r}_{x}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z}\right)-\left(\hat{r}_{y}^{2}+\hat{r}_{z}^{2}\right)\Gamma_{x}\right]

(433)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\left[\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}\right)-\left(1-\hat{r}_{x}^{2}\right)\Gamma_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\left[\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)-\Gamma_{x}\right],

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y},\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{y}\hat{r}_{z}\left[\Gamma_{z},\Gamma_{x}\right]-\hat{r}_{z}^{2}\left[\Gamma_{y},\Gamma_{x}\right]+\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{z}\left[\Gamma_{y},\Gamma_{z}\right]

(434)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}+2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{z}^{2}\Gamma_{z}+2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right),

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y},\Gamma_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{y}\left[\Gamma_{z},\Gamma_{y}\right]-\hat{r}_{z}\left[\Gamma_{y},\Gamma_{y}\right]

(435)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{y}\left(-2{\rm i}\Gamma_{x}\right)-0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x},

\displaystyle\left[\Gamma_{x},\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{y}\left[\hat{r}_{x}\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{x}\right]+\hat{r}_{y}\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{y}\right]+\hat{r}_{z}\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{z}\right]\right]

(436)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{y}\left[0+\hat{r}_{y}\left(2{\rm i}\Gamma_{z}\right)+\hat{r}_{z}\left(-2{\rm i}\Gamma_{y}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right),

\displaystyle\left[\Gamma_{x},\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{z}\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{x}\right]-\hat{r}_{x}\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{z}\right]

(437)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{z}\cdot 0-\hat{r}_{x}\left(-2{\rm i}\Gamma_{y}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y},

\displaystyle\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{y}\right]=2{\rm i}\Gamma_{z},

(438)

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right),\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}\left[\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r},\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right]=0,

(439)

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right),\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{z}\left[\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r},\Gamma_{x}\right]-\hat{r}_{x}^{2}\left[\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r},\Gamma_{z}\right]

(440)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{z}\left(2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}-2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}\right)-\hat{r}_{x}^{2}\left(2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}-2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\left[\left(\hat{r}_{z}^{2}+\hat{r}_{x}^{2}\right)\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\left[\left(1-\hat{r}_{y}^{2}\right)\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\left[\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right],

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right),\Gamma_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}\left[\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r},\Gamma_{y}\right]

(441)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{y}\right]+\hat{r}_{z}\left[\Gamma_{z},\Gamma_{y}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}\right).

By combining them according to their coefficients, we have

\displaystyle\left[A_{x},B_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2{\rm i}}{r^{3}}\left\{\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right]\hat{r}_{y}\left[k_{1}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)-\Gamma_{x}\right)+k_{2}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right)\right]\right.

(442)

\displaystyle+\left[\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)\right]\left[k_{1}\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)+k_{2}\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}+k_{3}\hat{r}_{x}\left(\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\left.+\left[\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]\left[-k_{1}\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}+k_{2}\Gamma_{z}+k_{3}\hat{r}_{x}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}\right)\right]\right\}.

Similarly, since

\displaystyle A_{y}=\frac{1}{r}\left[k_{1}\left(\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right)+k_{2}\Gamma_{y}+k_{3}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right],

(443)

\displaystyle B_{x}=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left\{\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right]\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{x}+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right)+\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\Gamma_{x}\right\},

(444)

we can calculate the nine fundamental commutators:

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z},\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\left[\hat{r}_{y}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z}\right)-\left(\hat{r}_{x}^{2}+\hat{r}_{z}^{2}\right)\Gamma_{y}\right]

(445)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\left[\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}\right)-\left(1-\hat{r}_{y}^{2}\right)\Gamma_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\left[\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)-\Gamma_{y}\right],

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z},\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{z}\hat{r}_{y}\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{z}\right]-\hat{r}_{z}^{2}\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{y}\right]+\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{z}\left[\Gamma_{z},\Gamma_{y}\right]

(446)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{z}\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}-2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{z}^{2}\Gamma_{z}-2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right),

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z},\Gamma_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\hat{r}_{x}\left[\Gamma_{z},\Gamma_{x}\right]=-2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y},

(447)

\displaystyle\left[\Gamma_{y},\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}\left[\hat{r}_{x}\left[\Gamma_{y},\Gamma_{x}\right]+\hat{r}_{y}\left[\Gamma_{y},\Gamma_{y}\right]+\hat{r}_{z}\left[\Gamma_{y},\Gamma_{z}\right]\right]

(448)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}\left[\hat{r}_{x}\left(-2{\rm i}\Gamma_{z}\right)+0+\hat{r}_{z}\left(2{\rm i}\Gamma_{x}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\left(\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right),

\displaystyle\left[\Gamma_{y},\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{y}\left[\Gamma_{y},\Gamma_{z}\right]=2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x},

(449)

\displaystyle\left[\Gamma_{y},\Gamma_{x}\right]=-2{\rm i}\Gamma_{z},

(450)

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right),\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{x}\right]=0,

(451)

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right),\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{y}^{2}\left[\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r},\Gamma_{z}\right]-\hat{r}_{y}\hat{r}_{z}\left[\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r},\Gamma_{y}\right]

(452)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\left[\left(\hat{r}_{y}^{2}+\hat{r}_{z}^{2}\right)\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\left[\left(1-\hat{r}_{x}^{2}\right)\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\left[\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right],

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right),\Gamma_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{y}\left[\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r},\Gamma_{x}\right]=2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}\right).

(453)

By combining them, we get

\displaystyle\left[A_{y},B_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2{\rm i}}{r^{3}}\left\{\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right]\hat{r}_{x}\left[k_{1}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)-\Gamma_{y}\right)+k_{2}\left(\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right)\right]\right.

(454)

\displaystyle+\left[\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)\right]\left[-k_{1}\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)+k_{2}\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}+k_{3}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\left.+\left[\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]\left[-k_{1}\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-k_{2}\Gamma_{z}+k_{3}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}\right)\right]\right\}.

As a result, we can have

\displaystyle\left[A_{x},B_{y}\right]-\left[A_{y},B_{x}\right]

(455)

\displaystyle=
