\displaystyle\frac{2{\rm i}}{r^{3}}\left\{\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right]\left[k_{1}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}\right)+k_{2}\left(\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right)\right]\right.

\displaystyle+\left[\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)\right]\left[2k_{1}\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\left.+\left[\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]\left[k_{1}\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}\right)+\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\Gamma_{z}-k_{3}\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2{\rm i}}{r^{3}}\left\{\left[k_{1}\left(2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right)+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)+\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}\right)\right.

\displaystyle+\left[k_{2}\left(2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right)+\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]\Gamma_{z}

\displaystyle\left.+\left[-k_{2}\left(2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right)+2k_{1}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right\}.

Remark 34
.

When we consider the following constraint

\displaystyle k_{1}={\frac{c\hbar}{2q}}a_{1},\;\;\;\;k_{2}=-\frac{c\hbar}{2q}a_{3},\;\;\;\;k_{3}=\frac{c\hbar}{2q}a_{3},\;\;\;q=g

\displaystyle(a_{1}-1)^{2}+a_{3}^{2}=1,

(456)

we can have

\displaystyle k_{2}+k_{3}=0,\;\;\;\;2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}=0,

(457)

which leads to
\vec{B}=0
, thus Eq. (
455
) is valid.
\blacksquare

(ii) Consider the curl term. Since

\displaystyle B_{x}=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left\{\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right]\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{x}+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right)+\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\Gamma_{y}\right\},

(458)

\displaystyle B_{y}=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left\{\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right]\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{y}+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)\left(\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right)+\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\Gamma_{y}\right\},

(459)

We can have

\displaystyle\partial_{x}B_{y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\left[\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)\left(\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right)+\left[\frac{2k_{1}}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}\frac{k_{2}k_{3}}{r^{2}}\right]\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{y}+\frac{2g}{\hbar c}\frac{k_{2}(k_{2}+k_{3})}{r^{2}}\Gamma_{y}\right]

(460)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(k_{2}+k_{3})\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)\left[-\frac{2\hat{r}_{x}}{r^{3}}\left(\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}\right)+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{-\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{z}}{r}\Gamma_{x}-\frac{1-\hat{r}_{x}^{2}}{r}\Gamma_{z}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right]\left[-\frac{2\hat{r}_{x}}{r^{3}}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{y}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)}{r}\hat{r}_{y}+\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\frac{-\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}}{r}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}(k_{2}+k_{3})\left(-\frac{2\hat{r}_{x}}{r^{3}}\Gamma_{y}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{3}}\left\{(k_{2}+k_{3})\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)\left[-3\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}+(3\hat{r}_{x}^{2}-1)\Gamma_{z}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right]\left[\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}-4\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]-\frac{4g}{\hbar c}k_{2}(k_{2}+k_{3})\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}\right\},

\displaystyle\partial_{y}B_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial y}\left[\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right)+\left[\frac{2k_{1}}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}\frac{k_{2}k_{3}}{r^{2}}\right]\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{x}+\frac{2g}{\hbar c}\frac{k_{2}(k_{2}+k_{3})}{r^{2}}\Gamma_{x}\right]

(461)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(k_{2}+k_{3})\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)\left[-\frac{2\hat{r}_{y}}{r^{3}}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right)+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{1-\hat{r}_{y}^{2}}{r}\Gamma_{z}-\frac{-\hat{r}_{y}\hat{r}_{z}}{r}\Gamma_{y}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right]\left[-\frac{2\hat{r}_{y}}{r^{3}}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{x}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)}{r}\hat{r}_{x}+\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\frac{-\hat{r}_{y}\hat{r}_{x}}{r}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}(k_{2}+k_{3})\left(-\frac{2\hat{r}_{y}}{r^{3}}\Gamma_{x}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{3}}\left\{(k_{2}+k_{3})\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)\left[(1-3\hat{r}_{y}^{2})\Gamma_{z}+3\hat{r}_{y}\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right]\left[\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-4\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]-\frac{4g}{\hbar c}k_{2}(k_{2}+k_{3})\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}\right\}.

So we can have

\displaystyle\partial_{x}B_{y}-\partial_{y}B_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{3}}\left(\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)\left[\Gamma_{z}-3\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]

(462)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{r^{3}}\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}+\frac{4g}{\hbar c}k_{2}(k_{2}+k_{3})\right]\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{3}}\left(\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)\left[\Gamma_{z}-3\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]-\frac{2}{r^{3}}\left[\frac{g(k_{1}^{2}-k_{2}k_{3}+2k_{2}^{2}+2k_{2}k_{3})}{\hbar c}-k_{1}\right]\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{3}}\left(\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)\left[\Gamma_{z}-3\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]-\frac{2}{r^{3}}\left[\frac{g\left({k_{1}^{2}+2k_{2}^{2}+k_{2}k_{3}}\right)}{\hbar c}-k_{1}\right]\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}\right).

(iii) Consider the electric-like field commutator term. We can have

\displaystyle\left[\varphi,E_{z}\right]=f_{1}(r)\left[\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right),E_{z}\right].

(463)

Because

\displaystyle{E}_{z}=-\frac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\hat{r}_{z}-\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right){\Gamma}_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\left[\frac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]-\frac{2gk_{2}}{\hbar c}\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}\right)_{z},

(464)

and consider that the
\hat{r}_{x}
term commutes with the potential, we only need to calculate the following two commutators:

\displaystyle[\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r},\;\Gamma_{z}]=-2{\rm i}(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x})=-2{\rm i}(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})_{z},

(465)

\displaystyle[\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r},\;\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}[(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}),\Gamma_{y}]-\hat{r}_{y}[(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}),\Gamma_{x}]

(466)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}[-2{\rm i}(\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z})]-\hat{r}_{y}[-2i(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2{\rm i}[\hat{r}_{z}(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y})-(\hat{r}_{x}^{2}+\hat{r}_{y}^{2})\Gamma_{z}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2{\rm i}[\hat{r}_{z}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z})-(1-\hat{r}_{z}^{2})\Gamma_{z}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2{\rm i}[\hat{r}_{z}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})-\Gamma_{z}].

Substituting these results into Eq. (
463
), we can have

\displaystyle[\varphi,E_{z}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}f_{1}(r)\biggl\{\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})_{z}+\frac{2gk_{2}}{\hbar c}\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}[\hat{r}_{z}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})-\Gamma_{z}]\biggr\}.

(467)

Remark 35
.

In summary, from above we have known that

\displaystyle\left[A_{x},B_{y}\right]-\left[A_{y},B_{x}\right]

(468)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2i}{r^{3}}\left\{\left[k_{1}\left(2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right)+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)+\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}\right)\right.

\displaystyle+\left[k_{2}\left(2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right)+\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]\Gamma_{z}

\displaystyle\left.+\left[-k_{2}\left(2k_{1}\left(\frac{g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\right)+2k_{1}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{2g}{\hbar c}k_{2}k_{3}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right\},

\displaystyle\partial_{x}B_{y}-\partial_{y}B_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{3}}\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)\left[\Gamma_{z}-3\hat{r}_{z}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right]-\frac{2}{r^{3}}\left[\kappa\left({k_{1}^{2}+2k_{2}^{2}+k_{2}k_{3}}\right)-k_{1}\right]\left(\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}\right),

(469)

\displaystyle[\varphi,E_{z}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle=2if_{1}(r)\biggl\{\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})_{z}+\frac{2gk_{2}f_{1}(r)}{\hbar cr}[\hat{r}_{z}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})-\Gamma_{z}]\biggr\}.

(470)

After substituting them into

\displaystyle\partial_{x}B_{y}-\partial_{y}B_{x}{-}\boxed{{\rm i}\,\frac{g}{\hbar c}\biggl(\Bigl[\varphi,E_{z}\Bigr]+[A_{x},B_{y}]-[A_{y},B_{x}]\biggr)}=0,

(471)

we can get three equations from the coefficients matching of
(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})_{z}
,
\Gamma_{z}
and
\hat{r}_{z}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})
, respectively. Explicitly, we have

(a) The coefficient of
(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})_{z}
:

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{r^{3}}\left(\frac{g\left({k_{1}^{2}+2k_{2}^{2}+k_{2}k_{3}}\right)}{\hbar c}-k_{1}\right)+\frac{2gf_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{\hbar cr}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{2g}{\hbar cr^{3}}\left[k_{1}\left(2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)-\frac{2gk_{2}k_{3}}{\hbar c}\right)+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)+\frac{2gk_{1}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)}{\hbar c}\right]=0.

(472)

(b) The coefficient of
\Gamma_{z}
:

\displaystyle\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{3}}\left(\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)-\frac{4g^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{\left(\hbar c\right)^{2}r}

\displaystyle+\frac{2g}{\hbar cr^{3}}\left[k_{2}\left(2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)-\frac{2gk_{2}k_{3}}{\hbar c}\right)+\frac{2gk_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)}{\hbar c}\right]=0.

(473)

(c) The coefficient of
\hat{r}_{z}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})
:

\displaystyle-\frac{3\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)}{r^{3}}\left(\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)+\frac{4g^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{\left(\hbar c\right)^{2}r}

\displaystyle+\frac{2g}{\hbar cr^{3}}\left[-k_{2}\left(2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)-\frac{2gk_{2}k_{3}}{\hbar c}\right)+2k_{1}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)-\frac{2gk_{2}k_{3}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)}{\hbar c}\right]=0.

(474)

\blacksquare

VI.2.2
Checking the Equation of the Electric-like Field

We now verify Eq. (
424a
).

(i) The divergence term:

\displaystyle\vec{E}=-\hat{r}f_{2}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\vec{\Gamma}-\left[f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right](\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\hat{r}-\frac{2gk_{2}f_{1}(r)}{\hbar cr}\left(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}\right),

(475)

where for convenience, we have denote
f_{j}^{\prime}(r)=\dfrac{\partial f_{j}(r)}{\partial r}
,
(j=1,2)
. We can have

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\vec{E}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\left\{-\hat{r}f_{2}^{\prime}\left(r\right)-\frac{f_{1}\left(r\right)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\vec{\Gamma}-\left[f_{1}^{\prime}\left(r\right)-\frac{f_{1}\left(r\right)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}-\frac{2gk_{2}f_{1}\left(r\right)}{\hbar cr}\left(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}\right)\right\}

(476)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\left[-f_{2}^{\prime}\left(r\right)\hat{r}\right]-\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\nabla\left[\frac{f_{1}\left(r\right)}{r}\right]-\nabla\cdot\left\{\left[f_{1}^{\prime}\left(r\right)-\frac{f_{1}\left(r\right)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{d}{dr}\left[r^{2}f_{2}^{\prime}\left(r\right)\right]-\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\left\{\hat{r}\frac{d}{dr}\left[\frac{f_{1}\left(r\right)}{r}\right]\right\}-\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{d}{dr}\left\{r^{2}\left[f_{1}^{\prime}\left(r\right)-\frac{f_{1}\left(r\right)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[f_{2}^{\prime\prime}\left(r\right)+\frac{2}{r}f_{2}^{\prime}\left(r\right)\right]-\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\left[\frac{f_{1}^{\prime}\left(r\right)}{r}-\frac{f_{1}\left(r\right)}{r^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle-\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\left\{\frac{2}{r}\left[f_{1}^{\prime}\left(r\right)-\frac{f_{1}\left(r\right)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]+\left[f_{1}^{\prime\prime}\left(r\right)-\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\left(\frac{f_{1}^{\prime}\left(r\right)}{r}-\frac{f_{1}\left(r\right)}{r^{2}}\right)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[f_{2}^{\prime\prime}\left(r\right)+\frac{2}{r}f_{2}^{\prime}\left(r\right)\right]-\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\left\{\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\left[\frac{f_{1}^{\prime}\left(r\right)}{r}-\frac{f_{1}\left(r\right)}{r^{2}}\right]+\frac{2f_{1}^{\prime}\left(r\right)}{r}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{2f_{1}\left(r\right)}{r^{2}}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)+f_{1}^{\prime\prime}\left(r\right)-\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\left[\frac{f_{1}^{\prime}\left(r\right)}{r}-\frac{f_{1}\left(r\right)}{r^{2}}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[f_{2}^{\prime\prime}\left(r\right)+\frac{2}{r}f_{2}^{\prime}\left(r\right)\right]-\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\left[f_{1}^{\prime\prime}\left(r\right)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}\left(r\right)-\frac{2f_{1}\left(r\right)}{r^{2}}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right].

(ii) The commutator term. We just need to calculate

\displaystyle\left[A_{x},E_{x}\right].

(477)

Because

\displaystyle A_{x}=\frac{1}{r}\left[k_{1}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right)+k_{2}\Gamma_{x}+k_{3}\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\right],

(478)

\displaystyle E_{x}=-\hat{r}_{x}f_{2}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\left[f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]-\frac{2gk_{2}f_{1}(r)}{\hbar cr}\left(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}\right)_{x}.

(479)

We need to calculate the following nine commutators:

\displaystyle\left[\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right),\Gamma_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{y}\left[\Gamma_{z},\Gamma_{x}\right]-\hat{r}_{z}\left[\Gamma_{y},\Gamma_{x}\right]

(480)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{y}\left(2{\rm i}\Gamma_{y}\right)-\hat{r}_{z}\left(-2{\rm i}\Gamma_{z}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z}\right),

\displaystyle\left[\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right),\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}\left[\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y},\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z}\right]

(481)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}\left\{\hat{r}_{y}\left[\Gamma_{z},\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}\right]-\hat{r}_{z}\left[\Gamma_{y},\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}\left\{\hat{r}_{y}\left(2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{y}-2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{x}\right)-\hat{r}_{z}\left(-2i\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{z}+2i\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{x}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\left[\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}-\hat{r}_{y}^{2}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{x}\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}^{2}\Gamma_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\left[\hat{r}_{x}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z}\right)-\left(\hat{r}_{y}^{2}+\hat{r}_{z}^{2}\right)\Gamma_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\left[\hat{r}_{x}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}\right)-\left(1-\hat{r}_{x}^{2}\right)\Gamma_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\left[\hat{r}_{x}^{2}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)-\hat{r}_{x}^{3}\Gamma_{x}-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{x}^{3}\Gamma_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\left[\hat{r}_{x}^{2}\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}\right],

\displaystyle\left[\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right),\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right)\right]=0,

(482)

\displaystyle\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{x}\right]=0,

(483)

\displaystyle\left[\Gamma_{x},\left(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}\right)\hat{r}_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}\left[\Gamma_{x},\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}+\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z}\right]

(484)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}\left\{\hat{r}_{x}\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{x}\right]+\hat{r}_{y}\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{y}\right]+\hat{r}_{z}\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{z}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}\left[0+\hat{r}_{y}\left(2{\rm i}\Gamma_{z}\right)+\hat{r}_{z}\left(-2{\rm i}\Gamma_{y}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right),

\displaystyle\left[\Gamma_{x},\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{y}\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{z}\right]-\hat{r}_{z}\left[\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{y}\right]

(485)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{y}\left(-2{\rm i}\Gamma_{y}\right)-\hat{r}_{z}\left(2{\rm i}\Gamma_{z}\right)=-2{\rm i}\left(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z}\right),

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{x}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}),\Gamma_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}\left[\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z},\Gamma_{x}\right]

(486)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{x}\left(\hat{r}_{y}[-2{\rm i}\Gamma_{z}]+\hat{r}_{z}[2{\rm i}\Gamma_{y}]\right)=-2{\rm i}\hat{r}_{x}(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}),

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}_{x}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}),(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y}),\hat{r}_{x}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\right]=-2{\rm i}\left[\hat{r}_{x}^{2}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}\right].

(487)

By combining them, we obtain

\displaystyle[A_{x},E_{x}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2{\rm i}}{r}\Biggl\{\left[\frac{2gk_{2}^{2}f_{1}(r)}{\hbar cr}-\frac{k_{1}f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right](\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{y}+\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{z})

(488)

\displaystyle-\left[k_{2}\left(f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right)-\frac{2gk_{1}k_{3}f_{1}(r)}{\hbar cr}-\frac{k_{3}f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]\hat{r}_{x}(\hat{r}_{y}\Gamma_{z}-\hat{r}_{z}\Gamma_{y})

\displaystyle-\left[k_{1}\left(f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{f_{1}(r)}{r}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right)-\frac{2gk_{2}k_{3}f_{1}(r)}{\hbar cr}\right]\left[\hat{r}_{x}^{2}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})-\hat{r}_{x}\Gamma_{x}\right]\Biggr\}.

From symmetry, it is obvious that the second and third terms vanish after summing over
x,y,z
. Thus, we have:

\displaystyle[A_{x},E_{x}]+[A_{y},E_{y}]+[A_{z},E_{z}]=-4i\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(k_{1}-\frac{2gk_{1}^{2}}{\hbar c}-\frac{2gk_{2}^{2}}{\hbar c}\right)f_{1}(r)(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}).

(489)

From Eq. (
424a
) we have

\displaystyle\vec{\nabla}\cdot\vec{E}\ {-}\boxed{{\rm i}\,\frac{g}{\hbar c}\Bigl([A_{x},E_{x}]+[A_{y},E_{y}]+[A_{z},E_{z}]\Bigr)}=0,

(490)

we can get two equations from matching coefficients. Explicitly, we have

(a) The coefficient of constant term:

\displaystyle f_{2}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{2}^{\prime}(r)=0

(491)

(b) The coefficient of
\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}
:

\displaystyle 4\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{g}{\hbar c}\left(k_{1}-\frac{2gk_{1}^{2}}{\hbar c}-\frac{2gk_{2}^{2}}{\hbar c}\right)f_{1}(r)+\left[f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{2f_{1}(r)}{r^{2}}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]=0

(492)

In fact, the
k_{3}
term does not affect the divergence equation of the electric field.

VI.3
Solving the Constraint Equations for
\vec{A}^{(1)}
and
\vec{A}^{(2)}

Now, we obtain a set of constraint equations for two functions
f_{1}(r)
,
f_{2}(r)
, the parameters
g
and
k_{j}
’s
(j=1,2,3)
, which are as follows

\displaystyle f_{2}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{2}^{\prime}(r)=0,

(493)

\displaystyle 4\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{g}{\hbar c}\left(k_{1}-\frac{2gk_{1}^{2}}{\hbar c}-\frac{2gk_{2}^{2}}{\hbar c}\right)f_{1}(r)+\left[f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{2f_{1}(r)}{r^{2}}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]=0,

(494a)

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{r^{3}}\left(\frac{g{\left(k_{1}^{2}+2k_{2}^{2}+k_{2}k_{3}\right)}}{\hbar c}-k_{1}\right)+\frac{2gf_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{\hbar cr}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{2g}{\hbar cr^{3}}\left[k_{1}\left(2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)-\frac{2gk_{2}k_{3}}{\hbar c}\right)+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)+\frac{2gk_{1}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)}{\hbar c}\right]=0,

(494b)

\displaystyle\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{3}}\left(\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)-\frac{4g^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{\left(\hbar c\right)^{2}r}

\displaystyle+\frac{2g}{\hbar cr^{3}}\left[k_{2}\left(2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)-\frac{2gk_{2}k_{3}}{\hbar c}\right)+\frac{2gk_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)}{\hbar c}\right]=0,

(494c)

\displaystyle-\frac{3\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)}{r^{3}}\left(\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)+\frac{4g^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{\left(\hbar c\right)^{2}r}

\displaystyle+\frac{2g}{\hbar cr^{3}}\left[-k_{2}\left(2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)-\frac{2gk_{2}k_{3}}{\hbar c}\right)+2k_{1}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)-\frac{2gk_{2}k_{3}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)}{\hbar c}\right]=0.

(494d)

Eq. (
493
) only involves the function
f_{2}(r)
, whose solution is

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r}+C_{0},

(495)

where
C_{0}
is trivial, one may set
C_{0}=0
. Eq. (
494a
)-Eq. (
494d
) involves the function
f_{1}(r)
and the parameters
g
and
k_{j}
’s. In this subsection, we shall determine them by considering the spin vector potential of
solution 1
(i.e.,
\vec{A}^{(1)}
) and the spin vector potential of
solution 2
(i.e.,
\vec{A}^{(2)}
).

VI.3.1
The Case of
\vec{A}^{(1)}

In this subsection, let us consider the spin vector potential of
solution 1
, for which

\displaystyle\{a_{1}=1,a_{2}=0,a_{3}=0\}.

(496)

Here we only consider the non-trivial case of
g\neq 0
(for the special case of
g=0
, we shall make a uniformly treatment in behind). Then in terms
\{\vec{\Gamma},\hat{r}\}
the spin vector potential can be written as

\displaystyle\vec{A}^{(1)}\equiv\vec{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\left[k_{1}(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})\right].

(497)

This means that

\displaystyle k_{1}={\frac{c\hbar}{2g}}a_{1}={\frac{c\hbar}{2g}},\;\;\;\;k_{2}=-\frac{c\hbar}{2g}a_{3}=0,\;\;\;\;k_{3}=\frac{c\hbar}{2g}a_{3}=0,

(498)

i.e., we have the constraint condition

\displaystyle\frac{2gk_{1}}{c\hbar}=1,

(499)

and

\displaystyle k_{2}=k_{3}=0,\;\;\;\frac{gk_{1}}{c\hbar}-1=-\frac{1}{2},

(500)

then Eq. (
494a
)-Eq. (
494d
) turn to

\displaystyle f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\dfrac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)=0,

(501)

which leads to

\displaystyle f_{1}(r)=\frac{C_{3}}{r}+C_{2}.

(502)

Remark 36
.

Due to

\displaystyle\tilde{g}=\frac{g}{c\hbar},\;\quad\tilde{k}=k_{1}\frac{2}{\hbar},

(503)

The condition (
510
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\frac{2gk_{1}}{c\hbar}=\frac{2}{c\hbar}gk_{1}=\frac{2}{c\hbar}(c\hbar\tilde{g})\left(\frac{\hbar}{2}\tilde{k}\right)=\tilde{g}\hbar\tilde{k}=1.

(504)

Thus we recover the result in Table
1
with the constraint
\tilde{g}\hbar\tilde{k}=1
.
\blacksquare

VI.3.2
The Case of
\vec{A}^{(2)}

In this subsection, let us consider the spin vector potential of
solution 2
, for which

\displaystyle\{a_{2}=-a_{1},(a_{1}-1)^{2}+a_{3}^{2}=1\}.

(505)

Similarly, here we only consider the non-trivial case of
g\neq 0
.
There are two special cases for Eq. (
505
). The first one

\displaystyle\{a_{1}=2,a_{2}=-2,a_{3}=0\}.

(506)

The second one is

\displaystyle\{a_{1}=0,a_{2}=0,a_{3}=0\}.

(507)

(i) The case of
\{a_{1}=2,a_{2}=-2,a_{3}=0\}
. In this case, in terms
\{\vec{\Gamma},\hat{r}\}
the spin vector potential can be written as

\displaystyle\vec{A}^{(1)}\equiv\vec{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\left[k_{1}(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})\right],

(508)

This means that

\displaystyle k_{1}={\frac{c\hbar}{2g}}a_{1}={\frac{c\hbar}{g}},\;\;\;\;k_{2}=-\frac{c\hbar}{2g}a_{3}=0,\;\;\;\;k_{3}=\frac{c\hbar}{2g}a_{3}=0,

(509)

i.e., we have the constraint condition

\displaystyle\frac{gk_{1}}{c\hbar}=1,

(510)

and

\displaystyle k_{2}=k_{3}=0,\;\;\;\frac{2gk_{1}}{c\hbar}-1=1.

(511)

Based on
k_{2}=k_{3}=0
and
g\neq 0
, from Eq. (
494a
)-Eq. (
494d
) we have

\displaystyle 4\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)f_{1}(r)+\left[f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{2f_{1}(r)}{r^{2}}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]=0,

(512a)

\displaystyle-\frac{2k_{1}}{r^{3}}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)+\frac{2gf_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{\hbar cr}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)+\frac{2g}{\hbar cr^{3}}\left[k_{1}\left(2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)\right)\right]=0.

(512b)

Furthermore, by considering
\dfrac{gk_{1}}{c\hbar}=1
, the above two equations become

\displaystyle f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{2f_{1}(r)}{r^{2}}=0

\displaystyle\frac{gf_{1}^{2}(r)}{c\hbar r}=0,

(513)

because
g\neq 0
, one has

\displaystyle f_{1}(r)=0.

(514)

Thus we recover the result in Table
1
with the constraint
\tilde{g}\hbar\tilde{k}=2
.

(ii) The case of
\{a_{1}=0,a_{2}=0,a_{3}=0\}
. In this case, in terms
\{\vec{\Gamma},\hat{r}\}
the spin vector potential can still be written as

\displaystyle\vec{A}^{(1)}\equiv\vec{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\left[k_{1}(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})\right],

(515)

but with
k_{1}=0
. After substituting
k_{1}=0
into Eqs. (
512a
) and (
512b
), we still have Eq. (
518a
). Then we have

f_{1}(r)=0
. Thus we recover the result in Table
1
with
\tilde{g}\neq 0
and
\tilde{k}=0
.

(iii) The general case
\{a_{2}=-a_{1},(a_{1}-1)^{2}+a_{3}^{2}=1\}
with
a_{3}\neq 0
. In this case, in terms
\{\vec{\Gamma},\hat{r}\}
the spin vector potential can be written as

\displaystyle\vec{A}=\frac{1}{r}\left[k_{1}(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})+k_{2}\vec{\Gamma}+k_{3}\hat{r}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\right].

(516)

This means that

\displaystyle k_{1}={\frac{c\hbar}{2g}}a_{1},\;\;\;\;k_{2}=-\frac{c\hbar}{2g}a_{3},\;\;\;\;k_{3}=\frac{c\hbar}{2g}a_{3}.

(517)

Based on
k_{2}+k_{3}=0
(or
k_{2}=-k_{3}
) and
g\neq 0
, from Eq. (
494a
)-Eq. (
494d
) we have

\displaystyle 4\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{g}{\hbar c}\left(k_{1}-\frac{2gk_{1}^{2}}{\hbar c}-\frac{2gk_{3}^{2}}{\hbar c}\right)f_{1}(r)+\left[f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{2f_{1}(r)}{r^{2}}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]=0,

(518a)

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{r^{3}}\left(\frac{g\left({k_{1}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}}\right)}{\hbar c}-k_{1}\right)+\frac{2gf_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{\hbar cr}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)+\frac{2g}{\hbar cr^{3}}\left[k_{1}\left(2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)+\frac{2gk_{3}^{2}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]=0,

(518b)

\displaystyle\frac{4g^{2}k_{3}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{\left(\hbar c\right)^{2}r}+\frac{2g}{\hbar cr^{3}}\left[-k_{3}\left(2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)+\frac{2gk_{3}^{2}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]=0,

(518c)

\displaystyle-\frac{4g^{2}k_{3}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{\left(\hbar c\right)^{2}r}+\frac{2g}{\hbar cr^{3}}\left[k_{3}\left(2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)+\frac{2gk_{3}^{2}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]=0.

(518d)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{g}{\hbar c}\left(k_{1}-\frac{2gk_{1}^{2}}{\hbar c}-\frac{2gk_{3}^{2}}{\hbar c}\right)f_{1}(r)+\left[f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{2f_{1}(r)}{r^{2}}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]=0,

(519a)

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{r^{3}}\left(\frac{g\left({k_{1}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}}\right)}{\hbar c}-k_{1}\right)+\frac{2gf_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{\hbar cr}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)+\frac{2g}{\hbar cr^{3}}\left[k_{1}\left(2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)+\frac{2gk_{3}^{2}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]=0,

(519b)

\displaystyle\frac{4g^{2}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{\left(\hbar c\right)^{2}r}-\frac{2g}{\hbar cr^{3}}\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)+\frac{2gk_{3}^{2}}{\hbar c}\right]=0.

(519c)

Because

\displaystyle\frac{g}{\hbar c}\left(k_{1}-\frac{2gk_{1}^{2}}{\hbar c}-\frac{2gk_{3}^{2}}{\hbar c}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-2\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)^{2}-2\left(\frac{gk_{3}}{\hbar c}\right)^{2}=\frac{a_{1}}{2}-2\left(\frac{a_{1}}{2}\right)^{2}-2\left(\frac{a_{3}}{2}\right)^{2}

(520)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(a_{1}-a_{1}^{2}-a_{3}^{2}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\left[a_{1}-a_{1}^{2}+\left[(a_{1}-1)^{2}-1\right]\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left[a_{1}-a_{1}^{2}+a_{1}^{2}-2a_{1}+1-1\right]=-\frac{a_{1}}{2},

\displaystyle\frac{g}{\hbar c}\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)+\frac{2gk_{3}^{2}}{\hbar c}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)^{2}-2\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)+2\left(\frac{gk_{3}}{\hbar c}\right)^{2}

(521)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)-\left[\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)-2\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)^{2}-2\left(\frac{gk_{3}}{\hbar c}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)+\frac{a_{1}}{2}=-\frac{a_{1}}{2}+\frac{a_{1}}{2}=0,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{g\left({k_{1}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}}\right)}{\hbar c}-k_{1}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\left(2\frac{g\left(k_{1}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}\right)}{\hbar c}-2k_{1}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\left[2k_{1}\left(\frac{gk_{1}}{\hbar c}-1\right)+\frac{2gk_{3}^{2}}{\hbar c}\right]=0.

(522)

Then we have

\displaystyle-\frac{2a_{1}}{r^{2}}f_{1}(r)+\left[f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{2f_{1}(r)}{r^{2}}\left(1-\frac{2gk_{1}}{\hbar c}\right)\right]=0,

(523a)

\displaystyle\frac{g^{2}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{\left(\hbar c\right)^{2}r}=0,

(523b)

i.e.,

\displaystyle f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{2f_{1}(r)}{r^{2}}=0,

(524a)

\displaystyle g^{2}f_{1}^{2}(r)=0.

(524b)

because
g\neq 0
, one has

\displaystyle f_{1}(r)=0.

(525)

VI.4
Solving the Constraint Equations for the General Spin Vector Potential
\vec{A}

The “general” spin vector potential is given by

\displaystyle\vec{A}=\frac{1}{r}\left[k_{1}(\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma})+k_{2}\vec{\Gamma}+k_{3}\hat{r}(\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r})\right].

(526)

For the “general” case, we means that we do not impose the constraint conditions on the parameters
k_{j}
’s
(j=1,2,3)
in the beginning.
From Eq. (
493
), we have determined the function
f_{2}(r)
as
f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r}
. Our task is to solve the function
f_{(}r)

for Eq. (
494a
)-Eq. (
494d
), but without imposing the constraint conditions on the parameters
k_{j}
’s in the beginning.

For convenience, let us denote
\kappa=\dfrac{g}{\hbar c}
, then Eq. (
494a
)-Eq. (
494d
) become

\displaystyle 4\frac{\kappa}{r^{2}}\left(k_{1}-2\kappa k_{1}^{2}-2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)f_{1}(r)+f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{2f_{1}(r)}{r^{2}}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)=0,

(527)

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{r^{3}}\left[\kappa\left(k_{1}^{2}+2k_{2}^{2}+k_{2}k_{3}\right)-k_{1}\right]+\frac{2\kappa f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[k_{1}\left(2k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)^{2}\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+2\kappa k_{1}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]=0,

(528)

\displaystyle\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{3}}\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}

\displaystyle+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[k_{2}\left(2k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)+2\kappa k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]=0,

(529)

\displaystyle-\frac{3\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)}{r^{3}}\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+\frac{4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}

\displaystyle+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[-k_{2}\left(2k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)+2k_{1}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]=0.

(530)

We shall divide the study into four cases: (1)
g=0
and
k_{1}=0
; (1)
g=0
and
k_{1}\neq 0
; (1)
g\neq 0
and
k_{1}=0
; (1)
g\neq 0
and
k_{1}\neq 0

VI.4.1
The Case of
\{g=0,k_{1}=0\}

In this case, from Eqs. (
527
)-(
VI.4
) we have

\displaystyle f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{2f_{1}(r)}{r^{2}}=0,

(531)

\displaystyle k_{2}+k_{3}=0.

(532)

Then we have

\displaystyle f_{1}(r)=\frac{C_{2}}{r^{2}}+C_{3}r,

(533)

with
k_{2}=-k_{3}
. Here
C_{2}
and
C_{3}
can be arbitrary real and complex numbers.

Remark 37
.

For convenience to
build
Tables
2
and
3
in Sec.
VI
, let us list the real and complex static solutions as follows.

Real Static Solution.—
We have the real static solution as

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=\frac{C_{2}}{r^{2}}+C_{3}r,\;\;\;\;k_{1}=0,\;\;\;k_{2}\in\mathbb{R},\;\;\;k_{3}=-k_{2},

(534)

where
\{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\}
are all real numbers.

Complex Static Solution.—
We have the complex static solution as

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=\frac{C_{2}}{r^{2}}+C_{3}r,\;\;\;\;k_{1}=0,\;\;\;k_{2}\in\mathbb{R},\;\;\;k_{3}=-k_{2},

(535)

where at least one of
\{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\}
is a complex number.
\blacksquare

VI.4.2
The Case of
\{g=0,k_{1}\neq 0\}

In this case, from Eqs. (
527
)-(
VI.4
) we have

\displaystyle f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{2f_{1}(r)}{r^{2}}=0,

(536)

\displaystyle\frac{2}{r^{3}}k_{1}=0,

(537)

\displaystyle k_{2}+k_{3}=0.

(538)

However, since
k_{1}\neq 0
, then Eq. (
537
) cannot be valid. Therefore, no solution for the case of
\{g=0,k_{1}\neq 0\}
.

VI.4.3
The Case of
\{g\neq 0,k_{1}=0\}

In this case, from Eqs. (
527
)-(
VI.4
) we have

\displaystyle 4\frac{\kappa}{r^{2}}\left(-2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)f_{1}(r)+f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{2f_{1}(r)}{r^{2}}=0,

(539)

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{r^{3}}\left[\kappa\left(2k_{2}^{2}+k_{2}k_{3}\right)\right]+\frac{2\kappa f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[-\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)^{2}\right]=0,

(540)

\displaystyle-\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{3}}-\frac{4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[k_{2}\left(-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)+2\kappa k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]=0,

(541)

\displaystyle\frac{3\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)}{r^{3}}+\frac{4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[-k_{2}\left(-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]=0.

(542)

By adding Eq. (
541
) and Eq. (
542
), one has

\displaystyle\frac{2\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)}{r^{3}}+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[2\kappa k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]=0,

(543)

\displaystyle\frac{\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)}{r^{3}}+\frac{\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[2\kappa k_{2}\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right]=0,

(544)

\displaystyle\frac{\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)}{r^{3}}+\frac{\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[4\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right]=0,

(545)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\frac{1+{4}\kappa^{2}k_{2}^{2}}{r^{3}}=0,

(546)

which leads to two possibilities:
(i)
k_{2}+k_{3}=0
;
(ii)
k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0
and
1+4\kappa^{2}k_{2}^{2}=0
;

3.1. The Case of
k_{2}+k_{3}=0

In this case, one has

\displaystyle k_{3}=-k_{2}.

(547)

Based on Eq. (
547
), after adding Eq. (
541
) and Eq. (
542
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}k_{2}\left(2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)=0,

(548)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}(r)+\frac{4\kappa^{2}}{r^{2}}k_{2}^{2}k_{3}=0,

(549)

i.e.,

\displaystyle k_{2}f_{1}^{2}(r)+\frac{1}{r^{2}}k_{2}^{2}k_{3}=0,

(550)

i.e.,

\displaystyle-k_{3}f_{1}^{2}(r)+\frac{1}{r^{2}}k_{3}^{3}=0,

(551)

i.e.,

\displaystyle k_{3}f_{1}^{2}(r)=\frac{1}{r^{2}}k_{3}^{3}.

(552)

There are two possibilities: (i)
k_{3}=0
; (ii)
k_{3}\neq 0
and
f_{1}^{2}(r)=\dfrac{k_{3}^{2}}{r^{2}}
.

(i) The Case of
k_{3}=0
.—
In this case, one has
k_{2}=k_{3}=0
. Then Eq. (
539
) and Eq. (
540
) become

\displaystyle f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{2f_{1}(r)}{r^{2}}=0,

(553)

\displaystyle\frac{2\kappa f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}=0,

(554)

which yield

\displaystyle f_{1}(r)=0.

(555)

(ii) The Case of
k_{3}\neq 0
and
f_{1}^{2}(r)=\dfrac{k_{3}^{2}}{r^{2}}
.—
In this case, one has
k_{2}=-k_{3}
. Then Eq. (
540
) becomes

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{r^{3}}\left[\kappa\left(k_{3}^{2}\right)\right]+\frac{2\kappa f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}=0,

(556)

i.e.,

\displaystyle f_{1}^{2}(r)=\frac{k_{3}^{2}}{r^{2}},

(557)

which is automatically valid. Similarly, Eq. (
539
) becomes

\displaystyle-\frac{8\kappa^{2}k_{3}^{2}}{r^{2}}f_{1}(r)+f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{2f_{1}(r)}{r^{2}}=0.

(558)

Let
f_{1}(r)=\dfrac{C}{r}
, with
C=\pm\sqrt{k_{3}^{2}}
, one has

\displaystyle-\frac{8\kappa^{2}k_{3}^{2}}{r^{2}}\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)+\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)^{\prime\prime}+\frac{2}{r}\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)^{\prime}-\frac{2}{r^{2}}\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)=0,

(559)

i.e.,

\displaystyle-\frac{8\kappa^{2}k_{3}^{2}}{r^{2}}\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)+\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)^{\prime\prime}+\frac{2}{r}\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)^{\prime}-\frac{2}{r^{2}}\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)=0,

(560)

i.e.,

\displaystyle-\frac{8\kappa^{2}k_{3}^{2}}{r^{3}}+\dfrac{2}{r^{3}}+\frac{2}{r}\times\dfrac{-1}{r^{2}}-\frac{2}{r^{3}}=0,

(561)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 8\kappa^{2}k_{3}^{2}+2=0,

(562)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa^{2}k_{3}^{2}+1=0,

(563)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa^{2}k_{2}^{2}+1=0.

(564)

This implies that when
k_{2}+k_{3}=0
and
1+4\kappa^{2}k_{2}^{2}=0
, the solution is
f_{1}(r)=\dfrac{C}{r}
, with
C=\pm\sqrt{k_{3}^{2}}
, and

\displaystyle k_{3}=-k_{2}=\mp\frac{{\rm i}}{2|\kappa|},

(565)

which is a pure imaginary number.

Remark 38
.

For convenience to
build
Tables
2
and
3
in Sec.
VI
, let us list the real and complex static solutions as follows.

Real Static Solution.—
We have the real static solution as

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=0,\;\;\;\;k_{1}=0,\;\;\;k_{2}=0,\;\;\;k_{3}=-k_{2}=0,

(566)

where
C_{1}
is a real number.

Complex Static Solution.—
We have the complex static solution as

(a)

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=0,\;\;\;\;k_{1}=0,\;\;\;k_{2}=0,\;\;\;k_{3}=-k_{2}=0,

(567)

with
C_{1}
is a complex number.

(b)

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=\frac{C}{r}=\frac{\pm\frac{{\rm i}}{2|\kappa|}}{r},\;\;\;\;k_{1}=0,\;\;\;k_{2}=\pm\frac{{\rm i}}{2|\kappa|},\;\;\;k_{3}=-k_{2}=\mp\frac{{\rm i}}{2|\kappa|},

(568)

where at least one of
\{C_{1},C,k_{2},k_{3}\}
is a complex number.
\blacksquare

3.2. The Case of
k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0
and
1+4\kappa^{2}k_{2}^{2}=0

In this case, we have

\displaystyle 4\kappa^{2}k_{2}^{2}=-1,

(569)

i.e.,

\displaystyle k_{2}^{2}=-\frac{1}{4\kappa^{2}},

(570)

i.e.,

\displaystyle k_{2}=\pm\frac{{\rm i}}{2|\kappa|},

(571)

namely,
k_{2}\neq 0
. From Eq. (
541
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}=-\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{3}}+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[k_{2}\left(-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)+2\kappa k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right],

(572)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}(r)=-\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{2}}+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{2}}\left[k_{2}\left(-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)+2\kappa k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right],

(573)

i.e.,

\displaystyle f_{1}^{2}(r)=k_{2}\left\{\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{2}}-\frac{2\kappa}{r^{2}}\left[k_{2}\left(-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)+2\kappa k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]\right\},

(574)

i.e.,

\displaystyle f_{1}^{2}(r)=k_{2}\left\{\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{2}}-\frac{2\kappa}{r^{2}}\left[-2\kappa k_{2}^{2}k_{3}+\left(2\kappa k_{2}^{2}+2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]\right\},

(575)

i.e.,

\displaystyle f_{1}^{2}(r)=k_{2}\left\{\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[-4\kappa^{2}k_{2}^{2}k_{3}+\left(4\kappa^{2}k_{2}^{2}+4\kappa^{2}k_{2}k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]\right\},

(576)

i.e.,

\displaystyle f_{1}^{2}(r)=k_{2}\left\{\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[k_{3}+\left(-1+4\kappa^{2}k_{2}k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]\right\},

(577)

i.e.,

\displaystyle f_{1}^{2}(r)=k_{2}\left\{\frac{k_{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[\left(1-4\kappa^{2}k_{2}k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]\right\},

(578)

i.e.,

\displaystyle f_{1}^{2}(r)=k_{2}\left\{\frac{k_{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[\left(1+\frac{k_{3}}{k_{2}}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]\right\},

(579)

i.e.,

\displaystyle f_{1}^{2}(r)=\left\{\frac{k_{2}^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]\right\},

(580)

i.e.,

\displaystyle f_{1}^{2}(r)=\frac{k_{2}^{2}+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)}{r^{2}},

(581)

i.e.,

\displaystyle f_{1}^{2}(r)=\frac{3k_{2}^{2}+3k_{2}k_{3}+k_{3}^{2}}{r^{2}}.

(582)

Hence the function
f_{1}(r)
has the structure of

\displaystyle f_{1}(r)=\frac{C}{r},

(583)

with

\displaystyle C^{2}=3k_{2}^{2}+3k_{2}k_{3}+k_{3}^{2}.

(584)

(i) If
C=0
, then we have

\displaystyle f_{1}(r)=0.

(585)

with

\displaystyle 3k_{2}^{2}+3k_{2}k_{3}+k_{3}^{2}=0,

(586)

i.e.,

\displaystyle k_{3}=\frac{-3\pm{\rm i}\sqrt{3}}{2}k_{2}.

(587)

(ii) If
C\neq 0
, then Eq. (
539
) becomes

\displaystyle 4\frac{\kappa}{r^{2}}\left(-2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)+\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)^{\prime\prime}+\frac{2}{r}\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)^{\prime}-\frac{2}{r^{2}}\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)=0,

(588)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\frac{\kappa}{r^{2}}\left(-2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)-\frac{2}{r^{2}}\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)=0,

(589)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa^{2}k_{2}^{2}=-1,

(590)

which is satisfied automatically.

Remark 39
.

For convenience to build Tables
2
and
3
in Sec.
VI
, let us list the real and complex static solutions as follows.

Real Static Solution.—
We do not have the real static solution because
k_{2}
is a complex number.

Complex Static Solution.—
We have the complex static solution as

(a)

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=0,\;\;\;\;k_{1}=0,\;\;\;k_{2}=\pm\frac{{\rm i}}{2|\kappa|},\;\;\;k_{3}=\frac{-3\pm{\rm i}\sqrt{3}}{2}k_{2},

(591)

with
C_{1}
is a real or complex number.

(b)

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=\frac{C}{r}=\frac{\pm\sqrt{3k_{2}^{2}+3k_{2}k_{3}+k_{3}^{2}}}{r},\;\;\;\;k_{1}=0,\;\;\;k_{2}=\pm\frac{{\rm i}}{2|\kappa|},,\;\;\;k_{3}\neq-k_{2},

(592)

with
C_{1}
is a real or complex number.
\blacksquare

VI.4.4
The Case of
\{g\neq 0,k_{1}\neq 0\}

In this case, from Eqs. (
527
)-(
VI.4
) we have

\displaystyle 4\frac{\kappa}{r^{2}}\left(k_{1}-2\kappa k_{1}^{2}-2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)f_{1}(r)+f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{2f_{1}(r)}{r^{2}}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)=0,

(593)

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{r^{3}}\left[\kappa\left(k_{1}^{2}+2k_{2}^{2}+k_{2}k_{3}\right)-k_{1}\right]+\frac{2\kappa f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[k_{1}\left(2k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)+\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)^{2}\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+2\kappa k_{1}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]=0,

(594)

\displaystyle\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{3}}\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}

\displaystyle+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[k_{2}\left(2k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)+2\kappa k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]=0,

(595)

\displaystyle-\frac{3\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)}{r^{3}}\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+\frac{4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}

\displaystyle+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[-k_{2}\left(2k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)+2k_{1}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]=0.

(596)

After adding Eq. (
VI.4.4
) and Eq. (
VI.4.4
), we have

\displaystyle-\frac{2\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)}{r^{3}}\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[2\kappa k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)+2k_{1}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]=0,

(597)

i.e.,

\displaystyle-\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+\kappa\left[2\kappa k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)+2k_{1}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]=0,

(598)

i.e.,

\displaystyle-\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+2\kappa\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left[\kappa k_{2}\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)+k_{1}\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right]=0,

(599)

i.e.,

\displaystyle-\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+2\kappa\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left[2\kappa k_{2}^{2}+2\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}\right]=0,

(600)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left\{(1-2\kappa k_{1})+2\kappa\left[2\kappa k_{2}^{2}+2\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}\right]\right\}=0,

(601)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left[1+4\kappa\left(\kappa k_{2}^{2}+\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}\right)\right]=0.

(602)

Then one has three possibilities: (i)
k_{2}=k_{3}=0
; (ii)
k_{2}=-k_{3}
and
k_{3}\neq 0
; (iii)
k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0
and
\left[1+4\kappa\left(\kappa k_{2}^{2}+\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}\right)\right]=0
.

4.1. The Case of
k_{2}=k_{3}=0

In this case, Eq. (
VI.4.4
) and Eq. (
VI.4.4
) are automatically satisfied. Eq. (
VI.4.4
) become

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{r^{3}}\left[\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}\right]+\frac{2\kappa f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[2k_{1}^{2}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)\right]=0,

(603)

i.e.,

\displaystyle-\frac{k_{1}}{r^{2}}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+\kappa f_{1}^{2}(r)\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)+\frac{\kappa}{r^{2}}\left[2k_{1}^{2}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)\right]=0,

(604)

i.e.,

\displaystyle-\frac{k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)}{r^{2}}+\kappa f_{1}^{2}(r)\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)+\frac{k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)}{r^{2}}\left[2\kappa k_{1}\right]=0,

(605)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\kappa f_{1}^{2}(r)\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)=\frac{k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)}{r^{2}}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right),

(606)

which leads to two possibilities: (i)
2\kappa k_{1}=1
; (ii)
2\kappa k_{1}-1\neq 0
and
f_{1}^{2}(r)=\dfrac{k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)/\kappa}{r^{2}}
.

(i) The case of
2\kappa k_{1}=1
. This situation also means
\left[1+4\kappa\left(\kappa k_{2}^{2}+\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}\right)\right]=0
with
k_{2}=0
. In this case, Eq. (
593
) becomes

\displaystyle f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)=0,

(607)

which yields

\displaystyle f_{1}(r)=\frac{C_{2}}{r}+C_{3}.

(608)

(ii) The case of
2\kappa k_{1}-1\neq 0
and
f_{1}^{2}(r)=\dfrac{k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)/\kappa}{r^{2}}
. For simplicity, we let

\displaystyle f_{1}(r)=\frac{C}{r},\;\;\;\;\;C=\pm\sqrt{k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)/\kappa}.

(609)

(ii-1) If
C=0
, then we have

\displaystyle\kappa k_{1}=1,

(610)

thus
f_{1}(r)=0
. In this situation, one may check that Eq. (
593
) is automatically satisfied.

(ii-2) If
C\neq 0
or
\kappa k_{1}\neq 1
, after substituting Eq. (
609
) into Eq. (
593
), we have

\displaystyle 4\frac{\kappa}{r^{2}}\left(k_{1}-2\kappa k_{1}^{2}\right)\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)+\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)^{\prime\prime}+\frac{2}{r}\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)^{\prime}-\frac{2}{r^{2}}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)=0,

(611)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\frac{\kappa}{r^{2}}\left(k_{1}-2\kappa k_{1}^{2}\right)\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)+\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)^{\prime\prime}+\frac{2}{r}\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)^{\prime}-\frac{2}{r^{2}}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)=0,

(612)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\frac{\kappa}{r^{2}}\left(k_{1}-2\kappa k_{1}^{2}\right)\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)+\dfrac{2}{r^{3}}+\frac{2}{r}\times\dfrac{-1}{r^{2}}-\frac{2}{r^{2}}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)=0,

(613)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\frac{\kappa}{r^{2}}\left(k_{1}-2\kappa k_{1}^{2}\right)\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)-\frac{2}{r^{2}}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)=0,

(614)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 2\kappa k_{1}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)-\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)=0,

(615)

i.e.,

\displaystyle-(2\kappa k_{1}-1)^{2}=0,

(616)

which cannot be valid for
2\kappa k_{1}-1\neq 0
. Thus there is no solution for this case.

Remark 40
.

For convenience to
build
Tables
2
and
3
in Sec.
VI
, let us list the real and complex static solutions as follows.

Real Static Solution.—
We have the real static solution as

(a)

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=\frac{C_{2}}{r}+C_{3},\;\;\;\;k_{1}=\frac{1}{2\kappa},\;\;\;k_{2}=0,\;\;\;k_{3}=0,

(617)

with
\{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}\}
are real numbers.

(b)

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=0,\;\;\;\;k_{1}=\frac{1}{\kappa},\;\;\;k_{2}=0,\;\;\;k_{3}=0,

(618)

with
C_{1}
is a real number.

Complex Static Solution.—
We have the complex static solution as

(a)

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=\frac{C_{2}}{r}+C_{3},\;\;\;\;k_{1}=\frac{1}{2\kappa},\;\;\;k_{2}=0,\;\;\;k_{3}=0,

(619)

with at least one of
\{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}\}
is complex number.

(b)

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=0,\;\;\;\;k_{1}=\frac{1}{\kappa},\;\;\;k_{2}=0,\;\;\;k_{3}=0,

(620)

with
C_{1}
is a complex number.
\blacksquare

4.2.
k_{2}=-k_{3}
and
k_{3}\neq 0

In this case, Eq. (
VI.4.4
) and Eq. (
VI.4.4
) reduces to

\displaystyle\frac{4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[-k_{2}\left(2k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)\right]=0,

(621)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}(r)=\frac{4\kappa k_{2}}{r^{2}}\left[k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right],

(622)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\kappa f_{1}^{2}(r)=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+\kappa k_{3}^{2}\right],

(623)

i.e.,

\displaystyle f_{1}^{2}(r)=\frac{\left(\kappa k_{1}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}^{2}-k_{1}\right)/\kappa}{r^{2}}.

(624)

After substituting Eq. (
624
) into Eq. (
VI.4.4
), we have

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{r^{3}}\left[\kappa\left(k_{1}^{2}+2k_{2}^{2}+k_{2}k_{3}\right)-k_{1}\right]+\frac{2\kappa f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[k_{1}\left(2k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)\right]=0,

(625)

i.e.,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{r^{3}}\left[\kappa\left(k_{1}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}\right)-k_{1}\right]+\frac{\kappa f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)+\frac{\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[k_{1}\left(2k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+2\kappa k_{3}^{2}\right)\right]=0,

(626)

i.e.,

\displaystyle-\left[\kappa\left(k_{1}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}\right)-k_{1}\right]+\left(\kappa k_{1}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}^{2}-k_{1}\right)\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)+\kappa k_{1}\left[2k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+2\kappa k_{3}^{2}\right]=0,

(627)

i.e.,

\displaystyle-\left[\kappa k_{1}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}^{2}-k_{1}\right]+\left(\kappa k_{1}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}^{2}-k_{1}\right)\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)+2\kappa k_{1}\left[\kappa k_{1}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}^{2}-k_{1}\right]=0,

(628)

which is satisfied automatically.

Now, the function
f_{1}(r)
has the structure

\displaystyle f_{1}(r)=\frac{C}{r},\;\;\;\;\;C=\pm\sqrt{\left(\kappa k_{1}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}^{2}-k_{1}\right)/\kappa}.

(629)

(i) If
C=0
, we have
f_{1}(r)=0
, one may check that Eq. (
593
) is automatically satisfied.
In this situation, we have

\displaystyle\kappa k_{1}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}^{2}-k_{1}=0.

(630)

Note: By considering

\displaystyle k_{1}={\frac{c\hbar}{2g}}a_{1},\;\;\;\;k_{2}=-\frac{c\hbar}{2g}a_{3},\;\;\;\;k_{3}=\frac{c\hbar}{2g}a_{3},\;\;\;\;\kappa=\frac{g}{c\hbar},

(631)

Eq. (
630
) becomes

\displaystyle\kappa^{2}k_{1}^{2}+\kappa^{2}k_{3}^{2}-\kappa k_{1}=0,

(632)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa^{2}k_{1}^{2}+4\kappa^{2}k_{3}^{2}-4\kappa k_{1}=0,

(633)

i.e.,

\displaystyle a_{1}^{2}+a_{3}^{2}-2a_{1}=0,

(634)

which is just the previous constraint

\displaystyle(a_{1}-1)^{2}+a_{3}^{2}=1.

(635)

For the real static solution, one can have the following parameterization

\displaystyle a_{1}=2\sin^{2}\theta,\;\;\;\;a_{3}=-2\sin\theta\cos\theta,

(636)

thus

\displaystyle k_{1}=\frac{c\hbar}{2g}a_{1}=\frac{c\hbar}{g}\sin^{2}\theta=\frac{1}{\kappa}\sin^{2}\theta,\;\;\;\;k_{3}=-\frac{1}{\kappa}\sin\theta\cos\theta,

(637)

with
k_{3}\neq 0
, i.e.,
\theta\neq\pi/2,3\pi/2
.

For the complex static solution, one can have the following parameterizations:

\displaystyle a_{1}=1\pm\cosh\vartheta,\;\;\;\;\ a_{3}={\rm i}\,\sinh\vartheta.

(638)

\displaystyle k_{1}=\frac{1}{2\kappa}(1\pm\cosh\vartheta),\;\;\;\;k_{3}=\frac{{\rm i}}{2\kappa}\sinh\vartheta,

(639)

with
\vartheta\neq 0
, or

\displaystyle a_{3}=\pm\cosh\vartheta,\;\;\;\;\ a_{1}=1+{\rm i}\sinh^{2}\vartheta.

(640)

\displaystyle k_{1}=\frac{1}{2\kappa}1+{\rm i}\sinh^{2}\vartheta,\;\;\;\;k_{3}=\pm\cosh\vartheta.

(641)

Here
\theta
and
\vartheta
are real numbers.

(ii) If
C\neq 0
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\kappa k_{1}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}^{2}-k_{1}\neq 0,

(642)

then from Eq. (
593
) we have

\displaystyle 4\frac{\kappa}{r^{2}}\left(k_{1}-2\kappa k_{1}^{2}-2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)+\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)^{\prime\prime}+\frac{2}{r}\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)^{\prime}-\frac{2}{r^{2}}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)=0,

(643)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\frac{\kappa}{r^{2}}\left(k_{1}-2\kappa k_{1}^{2}-2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)-\frac{2}{r^{2}}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)=0,

(644)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa\left(k_{1}-2\kappa k_{1}^{2}-2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)-2\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)=0,

(645)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 2\kappa\left(k_{1}-2\kappa k_{1}^{2}-2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)-\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)=0,

(646)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 2\kappa\left(2k_{1}-2\kappa k_{1}^{2}-2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)-1=0,

(647)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa\left(k_{1}-\kappa k_{1}^{2}-\kappa k_{3}^{2}\right)-1=0,

(648)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa\left(\kappa k_{1}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}^{2}-k_{1}\right)+1=0,

(649)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\kappa k_{1}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}^{2}-k_{1}=-\frac{1}{4\kappa}.

(650)

Thus

\displaystyle C=\pm\sqrt{\left(\kappa k_{1}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}^{2}-k_{1}\right)/\kappa}=\pm\sqrt{-\frac{1}{4\kappa^{2}}}=\pm\frac{{\rm i}}{2|\kappa|},

(651)

which is a complex number.

Remark 41
.

For convenience to
build
Tables
2
and
3
in Sec.
VI
, let us list the real and complex static solutions as follows.

Real Static Solution.—
We have the real static solution as

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=0,\;\;\;\;k_{1}=\frac{1}{\kappa}\sin^{2}\theta,\;\;\;\;k_{3}=-\frac{1}{\kappa}\sin\theta\cos\theta,\;\;\;k_{2}=-k_{3},\;\;\;k_{3}\neq 0,

(652)

with
C_{1}
is a real number. Here
k_{1}
and
k_{3}
satisfy the constraint
\kappa k_{1}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}^{2}-k_{1}=0
, i.e.,

k_{3}=\pm\sqrt{-\kappa k_{1}^{2}+(k_{1}/\kappa)}
.

Complex Static Solution.—
We have the complex static solution as

(a)

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=0,\;\;\;\;k_{1}=\frac{1}{\kappa}\sin^{2}\theta,\;\;\;\;k_{3}=-\frac{1}{\kappa}\sin\theta\cos\theta,\;\;\;k_{2}=-k_{3},\;\;\;k_{3}\neq 0,

(653)

with
C_{1}
is a complex number. Here
k_{1}
and
k_{3}
satisfy the constraint
\kappa k_{1}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}^{2}-k_{1}=0
.

(b)

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=0,\;\;\;\;k_{1}=\frac{1}{2\kappa}(1\pm\cosh\vartheta),\;\;\;\;k_{3}=\frac{{\rm i}}{2\kappa}\sinh\vartheta,\;\;\;k_{2}=-k_{3},\;\;\;k_{3}\neq 0,

(654)

with
C_{1}
is a real or complex number. Here
k_{1}
and
k_{3}
satisfy the constraint
\kappa k_{1}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}^{2}-k_{1}=0
.

(c)

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=0,\;\;\;\;k_{1}=\frac{1}{2\kappa}1+{\rm i}\sinh^{2}\vartheta,\;\;\;\;k_{3}=\pm\cosh\vartheta\;\;\;k_{2}=-k_{3},\;\;\;k_{3}\neq 0,

(655)

with
C_{1}
is a real or complex number. Here
k_{1}
and
k_{3}
satisfy the constraint
\kappa k_{1}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}^{2}-k_{1}=0
.

(d)

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=\frac{\pm\frac{{\rm i}}{2|\kappa|}}{r},\;\;\;\;\kappa k_{1}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}^{2}-k_{1}=-\frac{1}{4\kappa},\;\;\;k_{2}=-k_{3},\;\;\;k_{3}\neq 0,

(656)

with
C_{1}
is a real or complex number. Here
k_{1}
and
k_{3}
satisfy the constraint
\kappa k_{1}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}^{2}-k_{1}=-\frac{1}{4\kappa}
, i.e.,

k_{3}=\pm\sqrt{-\kappa k_{1}^{2}+\left(k_{1}-\frac{1}{4\kappa}\right)/\kappa}
.
\blacksquare

4.3. The Case of
k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0
and
\left[1+4\kappa\left(\kappa k_{2}^{2}+\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}\right)\right]=0

In this case, we have

\displaystyle 1+4\kappa\left(\kappa k_{2}^{2}+\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}\right)=0.

(657)

(i) If
k_{2}=0
and
k_{3}\neq 0
, then we have

\displaystyle 1+4\kappa\left(\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}\right)=0,

(658)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa^{2}k_{1}^{2}-4\kappa k_{1}+1=0,

(659)

i.e.,

\displaystyle(2\kappa k_{1}-1)^{2}=0,

(660)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 2\kappa k_{1}=1,

(661)

or

\displaystyle k_{1}=\frac{1}{2\kappa}.

(662)

From Eq. (
VI.4.4
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{k_{3}}{r^{3}}\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)=0,

(663)

which is automatically satisfied.

From Eq. (
VI.4.4
) we have

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{r^{3}}\left[\kappa\left(k_{1}^{2}+2k_{2}^{2}+k_{2}k_{3}\right)-k_{1}\right]+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[k_{1}\left(2k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)+2\kappa k_{1}k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]=0,

(664)

i.e.,

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{r^{3}}\left[\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}\right]+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[2k_{1}^{2}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)\right]=0,

(665)

i.e.,

\displaystyle-2k_{1}\left[\kappa k_{1}-1\right]+4\kappa k_{1}^{2}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)=0,

(666)

i.e.,

\displaystyle-1+2\kappa k_{1}=0,

(667)

which is valid.

From Eq. (
593
) we have

\displaystyle f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)=0,

(668)

which yields

\displaystyle f_{1}(r)=\frac{C_{2}}{r}+C_{3}.

(669)

(ii) If
k_{2}\neq 0
and
k_{3}\neq-k_{2}
, then we have

\displaystyle(2\kappa k_{2})^{2}+(2\kappa k_{1}-1)^{2}=0,

(670)

i.e.,

\displaystyle k_{2}^{2}=-\left(\frac{2\kappa k_{1}-1}{2\kappa}\right)^{2},

(671)

i.e.,

\displaystyle k_{2}=\pm\sqrt{-\left(\frac{2\kappa k_{1}-1}{2\kappa}\right)^{2}}=\pm{\rm i}\left|\frac{2\kappa k_{1}-1}{2\kappa}\right|.

(672)

Note that
k_{2}\neq 0
implies that
2\kappa k_{1}-1\neq 0
, and here
k_{2}
is generally a complex number.

From Eq. (
VI.4.4
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{3}}\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-\frac{4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}

\displaystyle+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[k_{2}\left(2k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)+2\kappa k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]=0,

(673)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\frac{4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}\left(r\right)}{r}=\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{3}}\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{3}}\left[k_{2}\left(2k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)+2\kappa k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right],

(674)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}(r)=\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{2}}\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+\frac{2\kappa}{r^{2}}\left[k_{2}\left(2k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-2\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)+2\kappa k_{2}\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right],

(675)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}(r)=\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{2}}\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+\frac{4\kappa k_{2}}{r^{2}}\left[\left(k_{1}\left(\kappa k_{1}-1\right)-\kappa k_{2}k_{3}\right)+\kappa\left(k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right],

(676)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}(r)=\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{2}}\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+\frac{4\kappa k_{2}}{r^{2}}\left[\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}-\kappa k_{2}k_{3}+2\kappa k_{2}^{2}+\kappa k_{2}k_{3}+\kappa k_{3}\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right],

(677)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}(r)=\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{r^{2}}\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+\frac{4\kappa k_{2}}{r^{2}}\left[\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}+2\kappa k_{2}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right].

(678)

Obviously, the function
f_{1}(r)
has the following structure

\displaystyle f_{1}(r)=\frac{C}{r},

(679)

and we shall analyze and simplify the number
C
later.

(ii-1) If
C=0
, then we have
f_{1}(r)=0
. In this situation, we have

\displaystyle C^{2}=\frac{(k_{2}+k_{3})\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+4\kappa k_{2}\left[\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}+2\kappa k_{2}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]}{4\kappa^{2}k_{2}}=0,

(680)

i.e.,

\displaystyle(k_{2}+k_{3})\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+k_{2}\left[4\kappa(\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}+2\kappa k_{2}^{2})+4\kappa^{2}k_{3}\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]=0,

(681)

i.e.,

\displaystyle(k_{2}+k_{3})\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+k_{2}\left[4\kappa(\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}+\kappa k_{2}^{2})+4\kappa^{2}k_{2}^{2}+4\kappa^{2}k_{3}\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]=0,

(682)

i.e.,

\displaystyle(k_{2}+k_{3})\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+k_{2}\left[-1+4\kappa^{2}(k_{2}+k_{3})^{2}\right]=0,

(683)

which is the constraint among
k_{1}
,
k_{2}
and
k_{3}
, with
k_{2}\neq 0
,
k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0
and
\left[1+4\kappa\left(\kappa k_{2}^{2}+\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}\right)\right]=0
.

(ii-2) If
C\neq 0
, after substituting Eq. (
679
) into Eq. (
593
), one has

\displaystyle 4\frac{\kappa}{r^{2}}\left(k_{1}-2\kappa k_{1}^{2}-2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)f_{1}(r)+f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2}{r}f_{1}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{2f_{1}(r)}{r^{2}}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)=0,

(684)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\frac{\kappa}{r^{2}}\left(k_{1}-2\kappa k_{1}^{2}-2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)+\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)^{\prime\prime}+\frac{2}{r}\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)^{\prime}-\frac{2}{r^{2}}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)\left(\dfrac{C}{r}\right)=0,

(685)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\frac{\kappa}{r^{2}}\left(k_{1}-2\kappa k_{1}^{2}-2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)+\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)^{\prime\prime}+\frac{2}{r}\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)^{\prime}-\frac{2}{r^{2}}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)=0,

(686)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\frac{\kappa}{r^{2}}\left(k_{1}-2\kappa k_{1}^{2}-2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)+\dfrac{2}{r^{3}}+\frac{2}{r}\times\dfrac{-1}{r^{2}}-\frac{2}{r^{2}}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)=0,

(687)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\frac{\kappa}{r^{2}}\left(k_{1}-2\kappa k_{1}^{2}-2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)-\frac{2}{r^{2}}\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)\left(\dfrac{1}{r}\right)=0,

(688)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 2\kappa\left(k_{1}-2\kappa k_{1}^{2}-2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)-\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)=0,

(689)

i.e.,

\displaystyle-2\kappa\left(k_{1}-2\kappa k_{1}^{2}-2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)+\left(1-2\kappa k_{1}\right)=0,

(690)

i.e.,

\displaystyle(2\kappa k_{2})^{2}+(2\kappa k_{1}-1)^{2}=0,

(691)

which is valid automatically.

Now let us come to see what is the number
C
.

Analysis 1.—
If
k_{3}=0
, then Eq. (
678
) becomes

\displaystyle 4\kappa^{2}k_{2}f_{1}^{2}(r)=\frac{k_{2}}{r^{2}}\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+\frac{4\kappa k_{2}}{r^{2}}\left[\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}+2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right],

(692)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa^{2}f_{1}^{2}(r)=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+\frac{4\kappa}{r^{2}}\left[\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}+2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right],

(693)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa^{2}f_{1}^{2}(r)=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[2\kappa k_{1}-1+4\kappa\left(\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}+2\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)\right].

(694)

Due to

\displaystyle 1+4\kappa\left(\kappa k_{2}^{2}+\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}\right)=0,

(695)

one has
i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa^{2}f_{1}^{2}(r)=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[2\kappa k_{1}-1+4\kappa\left(\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}+\kappa k_{2}^{2}\right)+4\kappa^{2}k_{2}^{2}\right],

(696)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 4\kappa^{2}f_{1}^{2}(r)=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[2\kappa k_{1}-2+4\kappa^{2}k_{2}^{2}\right],

(697)

i.e.,

\displaystyle 2\kappa^{2}f_{1}^{2}(r)=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left[\kappa k_{1}-1+2\kappa^{2}k_{2}^{2}\right].

(698)

Thus
C
satisfies

\displaystyle C^{2}=\frac{\kappa k_{1}-1+2\kappa^{2}k_{2}^{2}}{2\kappa^{2}}.

(699)

Analysis 2.—
If
k_{3}\neq 0
, then from Eq. (
678
) we can have

\displaystyle C^{2}=\frac{(k_{2}+k_{3})\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+4\kappa k_{2}\left[\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}+2\kappa k_{2}^{2}+\kappa k_{3}\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]}{4\kappa^{2}k_{2}},

(700)

i.e.,

\displaystyle C^{2}=\frac{(k_{2}+k_{3})\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+k_{2}\left[4\kappa(\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}+2\kappa k_{2}^{2})+4\kappa^{2}k_{3}\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]}{4\kappa^{2}k_{2}},

(701)

i.e.,

\displaystyle C^{2}=\frac{(k_{2}+k_{3})\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+k_{2}\left[4\kappa(\kappa k_{1}^{2}-k_{1}+\kappa k_{2}^{2})+4\kappa^{2}k_{2}^{2}+4\kappa^{2}k_{3}\left(2k_{2}+k_{3}\right)\right]}{4\kappa^{2}k_{2}},

(702)

i.e.,

\displaystyle C^{2}=\frac{(k_{2}+k_{3})\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+k_{2}\left[-1+4\kappa^{2}(k_{2}+k_{3})^{2}\right]}{4\kappa^{2}k_{2}},

(703)

which reduces to Eq. (
699
) when
k_{3}
becomes 0.

Remark 42
.

For convenience to build Tables
2
and
3
in Sec.
VI
, let us list the real and complex static solutions as follows.

Real Static Solution.—
We have the real static solution as

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=\frac{C_{2}}{r}+C_{3},\;\;\;\;k_{1}=\frac{1}{2\kappa},\;\;\;\;k_{2}=0,\;\;\;k_{3}\neq 0,

(704)

with
\{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}\}
are real numbers. Here the constraint is
2\kappa k_{1}=1
.

Complex Static Solution.—
We have the complex static solution as

(a)

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=\frac{C_{2}}{r}+C_{3},\;\;\;\;k_{1}=\frac{1}{2\kappa},\;\;\;\;k_{2}=0,\;\;\;k_{3}\neq 0,

(705)

with at least one of
\{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}\}
is complex number. Here the constraint is
2\kappa k_{1}=1
.

(b)

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=0,\;\;\;\;k_{1}\neq\frac{1}{2\kappa},\;\;\;\;k_{2}=\pm{\rm i}\left|\frac{2\kappa k_{1}-1}{2\kappa}\right|,

\displaystyle(k_{2}+k_{3})\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+k_{2}\left[-1+4\kappa^{2}(k_{2}+k_{3})^{2}\right]=0,

(706)

with
C_{1}
is a real or complex number. Here
k_{1}
,
k_{2}
and
k_{3}
satisfy the constraint
(k_{2}+k_{3})\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+k_{2}\left[-1+4\kappa^{2}(k_{2}+k_{3})^{2}\right]=0
.

(c)

\displaystyle f_{2}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r},\;\;\;\;f_{1}(r)=\frac{C}{r},\;\;\;\;k_{1}\neq\frac{1}{2\kappa},\;\;\;\;k_{2}=\pm{\rm i}\left|\frac{2\kappa k_{1}-1}{2\kappa}\right|,\;\;\;\;\;k_{3}\;is\;arbitrary,

(707)

with
C_{1}
and
k_{3}
are real or complex numbers,
C^{2}=\dfrac{(k_{2}+k_{3})\left(2\kappa k_{1}-1\right)+k_{2}\left[-1+4\kappa^{2}(k_{2}+k_{3})^{2}\right]}{4\kappa^{2}k_{2}}
.
\blacksquare

VII
Conclusion and Discussion

In this work, we have achieved a complete classification of static solutions to the vacuum SU(2) Yang-Mills equations that carry an explicit dependence on spin degrees of freedom. By generalizing the vector potential extraction approach (VPEA) from the Abelian to the non-Abelian case, we derived the most general form of the spin vector potential compatible with the angular momentum algebra. This led to the systematic ansatz of Eq. (2), whose substitution into the Yang-Mills field equations yielded a closed set of constraint equations. Solving these constraints case by case produced the rich families of real and complex static solutions summarized in Tables II and III.

Summary of Key Results.—

•

Recovery of known solutions
: Our classification successfully reproduces the simple SU(2) static solution of Ref.
Zhou2025
as the special case
k_{2}=k_{3}=0
with
2\kappa k_{1}=1
or
2
. This serves as a consistency check for our general framework.

•

New real static solutions
: Beyond the known solution, we discovered several families of real solutions with non-zero
k_{2},k_{3}
and non-trivial radial functions
f_{1}(r)
. These configurations demonstrate that the internal spin structure allows for significantly richer vacuum configurations than those captured by standard point-source models
Faddeev1999
. Notably, many of these new solutions satisfy the VPEA-derived constraint
k_{2}+k_{3}=0
and exhibit a purely radial, Coulomb-like electric field with a vanishing magnetic field.

•

Complex static solutions
: By relaxing the reality condition, we uncovered a rich set of complex solutions. While their direct physical interpretation remains open, such complex classical configurations are increasingly recognized as important in modern theoretical frameworks, including the analytic continuation of gauge theories
Witten2010
and resurgence theory

Dunne2012
;
Basar2013
;
Aniceto2019
. They also echo earlier explorations of complex solutions in topologically massive gauge theories
Oh1990PLB
.

•

Role of the VPEA constraints
: The angular-momentum constraints derived from the VPEA, namely
(a_{1}-1)^{2}+a_{3}^{2}=1
(equivalently
\kappa(k_{1}^{2}+k_{3}^{2})-k_{1}=0
and
k_{2}+k_{3}=0
), emerge as a special subclass of our general solutions. When these constraints are imposed, the magnetic field vanishes identically (
\vec{B}=0
), and the solution reduces to a pure gauge configuration (up to the scalar potential). This provides a clear physical interpretation of the VPEA conditions.

Physical Implications.—
The existence of these spin-dependent static solutions suggests a novel phenomenon in non-Abelian gauge theory: The gauge field can support “Coulomb-like” interactions that couple directly to the internal spin of a test particle. This is reminiscent of the classical color-electric fields generated by non-Abelian point sources
Oh1986PRD
, but with a crucial difference: here, the interaction acquires a directional dependence through the
\vec{\Gamma}\cdot\hat{r}
and
\hat{r}\times\vec{\Gamma}
structures.

A natural question arises: Can these solutions be interpreted as describing a “spin-charged” object? If so, how would such an object differ from the well-studied Wu-Yang
WuYang1969
or ’t Hooft-Polyakov
tHooft1974
monopoles? Unlike those topologically stable solitons, our solutions are not necessarily finite-energy or topologically protected. Nevertheless, they may represent important saddle-point configurations in the path integral, contributing to non-perturbative phenomena such as vacuum tunneling or confinement. The fact that some solutions exhibit a vanishing magnetic field while others do not suggests a possible phase structure depending on the parameters
k_{i}
.

Limitations and Future Directions.—
Several natural extensions of this work present themselves:

1.

Higher spins
: Our analysis was restricted to spin-
1/2
for simplicity. Extending the VPEA to higher spins (using the appropriate spin matrices) is straightforward in principle, though the algebraic constraints will become more complex. Such an extension could reveal spin-dependent effects that scale with the spin quantum number.

2.

Time-dependent generalizations
: We have considered only static fields. Relaxing this assumption could uncover non-Abelian progressive waves
Oh1985JMP
or other wave-like solutions recently discussed in the literature
Rabinowitch2026
. The VPEA framework might offer a new perspective on constructing such dynamical solutions.

3.

Physical properties of the new solutions
: It would be worthwhile to compute the classical energy density, total energy, and possible topological charges of the new real solutions. Are any of them normalizable? Could they serve as backgrounds for semi-classical quantization
Callan1978
;
Prasad1975
?

4.

Interpretation of complex solutions
: The complex solutions we found require further physical interpretation. They might be relevant in the context of Euclidean instantons
BPST1975
, dyon configurations
JuliaZee1975
, or non-Hermitian extensions of Yang-Mills theory. Their role in resurgence theory, where complex “ghost” instantons contribute to transseries expansions, is particularly intriguing
Dunne2012
;
Basar2013
;
Aniceto2019
.

Broader Applicability of the VPEA.—
Finally, we note that the vector potential extraction approach has proven to be a powerful heuristic tool for generating gauge potentials that encode angular momentum information. Its success in the Abelian case—reproducing the AB effect
2005QParadox
, uniform magnetic field
1999Jackson
, Dirac monopole
Dirac1931
, and Wu-Yang monopole
WuYang1975
;
WuYang1976
—and now in the non-Abelian SU(2) case, suggests that the method may have even wider applicability. One could, for example, apply the same idea to higher-dimensional gauge theories, to other gauge groups such as SU(3), or explore its potential connections to the algebraic matrix constructions underlying the ADHM
ADHM1978
and Nahm
Nahm1982
methods.

Concluding Remark.—
In summary, this work provides a complete and systematic classification of static SU(2) Yang-Mills solutions with a spin-dependent vector potential derived from the VPEA. The resulting families—both real and complex—significantly expand the known landscape of exact solutions and open up new avenues for exploring the role of spin in non-Abelian gauge theories, both at the classical and, potentially, the quantum level.
